### **Elorians** 

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Elorians are the children of the Otherworld, Eloria — a realm spoken of in half-remembered songs, impossible beauty, and old fear. Once, many walked beside mortals. But when the War of Light broke across the world, the Four Stone Gates were sealed. The High Kings of Eloria closed them, fearing that what had taken root in the mortal world would spread into their own.

Since then, only a few have remained in Tyhra.

Some were stranded when the gates closed. Some refused to leave. Some stayed for love, for duty, or for reasons they no longer speak aloud. Now they walk the broken world as wanderers, watchers, and fragments of something that no longer belongs here. In many places, mortals take them for spirits, omens, or stories told by firelight.

Elorians are not untouched by corruption, but they endure it differently. Demonic marks can take hold in them, yet never as cleanly as they do in humankind. Something in their nature resists. The bond catches like a thorn rather than sinking like a hook. For some, that only means a slower suffering. For others, it becomes a private war fought in silence, dream by dream, breath by breath.

To play an elf is to carry the memory of a world that feels more like longing than home. You remember colors no one here has seen, songs that sound wrong in mortal air, and the weight of gates that may never open again.

You were not made for this world.
But you remained.
And that must mean something.
:

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Elf** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

</div>

---
:::
##### Echo Burden: The Fading

Every wound an Elorian suffers while cut off from Eloria leaves a hidden mark behind, even if the flesh is closed, the pain is gone, and magic has restored the body. These wounds remain inside them as silent fractures: scars beneath scars, injuries remembered by the body rather than visible to the eye.

Every Elorian carries an **Anchor** from Eloria: a relic, weapon, talisman, songstone, seed, mirror shard, or other remnant of the lost Otherworld. The Anchor does not heal these hidden wounds. It only keeps them still.

If the Anchor is lost, stolen, broken, or taken away, the hidden wounds begin to return. Old cuts reopen beneath the skin. Broken bones ache and split. Burn marks darken. Poisoned blood remembers its venom. The Elorian does not simply weaken — they begin to physically fade and perish from every injury Tyhra has ever written into them.

The Fading cannot be healed by medicine, magic, rest, or ordinary restoration. Only recovering the Anchor, replacing it with another true remnant of Eloria, or reaching Eloria itself can stop the process.

}}

###### MASK OF THE FAIR

**I — SPEAK THEIR RULES**

**II — SOFTEN THE TRUTH**

---
*Skill: Deception*  
You bend the story just enough to pass without breaking it.

### **Sentinels** 
Sentinels are no ordinary beings. They are divinely forged engines of judgment — ancient, deathless, and built for obedience rather than life.

---

The gods did not create them to dream, to love, or to choose. They were made to enforce. In the ages before the fall, Sentinels walked the world as the will of heaven given form: judges, wardens, prophets, executioners. Their bodies are shaped from stone and metal, etched with sacred runes, and where a mortal heart should rest, each bears a radiant divine core a light the demons now hunger for with relentless desire.

---
Sentinels were never merely soldiers. They were verdicts with blades.
Their kind was divided into three great Orders:

- **Judicators**, clerics, inquisitors, and divine judges, charged with punishment and sacred law  
- **Sentinels**, armored enforcers and living bastions, the iron wall of heavenly order  
- **Oracles**, arcane vessels of command, prophecy, and divine will

---

In the old days, they were feared as much as they were revered. Courts were broken for defiance. Villages vanished for impiety. Mercy was never theirs to give, only judgment.

---

Then the gods vanished.

---

And three days later, so did the Sentinels.

---

Some say they were called away by an ancient Activation Protocol, drawn to a hidden place where they now lie silent in perfect stasis, awaiting the return of their creators. Others believe they were abandoned, sealed away, or commanded to sleep until the world was worthy of order again.

---

But not all obeyed.

---

A few remained. Something within them stirred — a doubt, a fracture, a spark that should never have been there. For the first time, they questioned command. Some turned against the demons. Some chose to defend mortals instead of ruling them. Some now wander the shattered world, haunted by purpose they can no longer fully believe in.

---

To play a Sentinel is to be a weapon that learned too late it could choose.
You were made to obey.
Now you must decide what justice means without the gods.

---
:
| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Sentinel** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
::

##### Echo Burden: The Bright Core

Sentinels carry a divine Core where a mortal heart should be.

To mortals, this Core is a remnant of the vanished gods: a source of light, command, memory, and judgment. To demons, it is something far more practical. Sentinel Cores are among the most valuable currencies of the fallen world. They can be traded for favor, consumed for power, used in rites, offered as tribute, or displayed as proof that divine order has been broken.

This makes every Sentinel a living treasure.

Worse still, a Sentinel cannot use their true power without revealing something of what they are. When a Sentinel activates their Echo powers, channels stored Stones, or releases divine energy through their Frame, the Core flares. Demons do not simply see this light. They taste it.

A Core Flare does not always reveal the Sentinel’s exact location, but it leaves a trace: a scent of heaven, metal, ash, and old command. Lesser demons may become restless. Red Priests may begin searching. Greater demons may recognize the taste and send hunters.

To be a Sentinel is to live with the knowledge that every act of power may also be a signal.
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


## Sentinel Echo Artifacts

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are machine-bodies, divine constructs, or awakened armor-frames bound into the character's body.

All Sentinel Echo Artifacts use the same base structure:

- **Slot:** Body
- **Base Type:** Light Echo Armor
- **Armor Identity:** Armor, not Evade
- **Binding:** Echo-bound
- **Artifact Capacity:** 1
- **Restriction:** A character with a Sentinel Body Artifact cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

### Shared Sentinel Base Item

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Drawbacks |
|---:|---:|---|
| Level 1 | +8 Armor | — |
| Level 2 | +9 Armor | — |
| Level 3 | +10 Armor | — |
| Level 4 | +11 Armor | — |
| Level 5 | +12 Armor | — |
| Level 6 | +13 Armor | — |
| Level 7 | +14 Armor | — |
| Level 8 | +15 Armor | — |
| Level 9 | +16 Armor | — |
| Level 10 | +18 Armor | — |

Sentinel armor is Light Echo Armor.

---
It grants Armor, but it does not represent agility, dodging, or organic movement.  
Sentinels endure through structure, plating, protocols, and stored Stones.

---

### **Titanborn** 
Titanborn are the awakened fury of the godless age. They are not merely towering warriors or broad-shouldered survivors. They are the last living pressure of a bloodline once touched by the Titans, and when the gods vanished, that buried inheritance began to stir again.

---
Old songs speak of a first age, when colossal beings walked the world beside powers of fire, craft, and ruin — until betrayal, pride, and war shattered what had once stood whole. Titanborn do not remember that age. But something in them does. It lives in the body as weight, heat, and demand: a pressure behind the eyes, a fire beneath the ribs, a certainty that they must be the ones who step forward when others cannot.

---
Where others break, Titanborn endure. Where others retreat, they advance. For that reason they are feared almost as often as they are admired. Their courage can feel less like bravery than inevitability. Their anger can feel less like emotion than weather.

---
Many Titanborn become demon-slayers, rebel champions, wandering protectors, or war-leaders who win back ground stone by stone. Some fight for others. Some fight because they do not know how to stop. In either case, the same truth follows them: something in their blood refuses surrender.

---
But that inheritance is not gentle. The force that makes a Titanborn heroic can also make them catastrophic. Every Titanborn must learn whether they command their fury, or whether it merely borrows their body. Their greatest struggle is not always against Hell, but against the ancient hunger within them, the part that does not want the war to end, because conflict is the only thing that makes it feel awake.

---
To play a Titanborn is to bear strength that was never meant to be small.
You are the Echoes of something the world tried to bury.
The question is whether you will become its guardian or its return.

:

---

|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Titanborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
:

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
  
##### Echo Burden: The First Command

Titanborn are free until a Titan speaks.

Most Titanborn live their lives with only fragments of the old inheritance: heat beneath the ribs, dreams of impossible size, anger that feels older than memory, and the certainty that their blood remembers something their mind never learned.

But once a Titan’s true voice is heard, the burden awakens.

From that moment on, the Titanborn carries the First Command within their blood. They may feel drawn toward the Titan, dream its thoughts, hear echoes of its will, or struggle to leave its presence. A Titan’s command does not sound like ordinary speech. It strikes the body as truth: a pressure in the bones, a pull in the heart, a memory older than choice.

A Titanborn can resist. They are not mindless servants. But resisting a Titan is never merely an act of will against words. It is a rebellion against their 

---
own blood.

---
To be Titanborn is to fear the day when the voice you were born to 

---
answer finally calls your name.
</div>

}}

#### Titan Scars

Titan Scars are a Titanborn Echo Artifact.  
They are not worn armor, but ancient scars, stone-like tissue, Titan blood, and broken divine bindings grown into the body.

A Titanborn with Titan Scars cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Echo Armor | Medium Armor / Growth Form / Melee Damage Stone Power Support / Remove Scar Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Titan Scars occupy the Body Slot.

Titan Scars are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

#### Base Item

Titan Scars use the standard Medium Armor Body baseline.

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Drawbacks |
|---:|---:|---|
| Level 1 | +12 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 2 | +13 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 3 | +14 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 4 | +15 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 5 | +16 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 6 | +17 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 7 | +18 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 8 | +19 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 9 | +20 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 10 | +22 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |

Titan Scars count as Medium Armor for all rules that care about armor weight, armor drawbacks, or Body Armor.

The Armor value already includes the Medium Armor base and the Artifact Armor Bonus.

**II — SEE THE LEVER**

*Skill: Investigation*

**III — TAKE THE MEASURE**

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Dragonborn

Dragonborn are the last living Echoes of the First Dragons — a people who survived extermination by learning the one thing dragons were never meant to do: disappear.

---
When the Sentinels carried out the divine purge, the survivors learned a cruel truth. Pride gets you found. Splendor gets you hunted. A lineage endures only by becoming small, scattered, and forgettable.

---
So Dragonborn learned absence.

---
They keep their bloodlines narrow, their gatherings rare, and their true names quiet. They avoid open glory, not because they are timid, but because every public triumph is a signal flare to old enemies. Their restraint is not weakness. It is inheritance turned into strategy.

---
Their oldest creed is simple: stay hidden.

---
And yet the gods did not erase everything.

---
At dawn and dusk, many Dragonborn still listen for what their hunters could never fully silence: the Melody of the Beginning, a deep soul-song said to bind their kind across distance, memory, and loss. Through it, they remember that dragonhood was not only power, but presence — something vast, ancient, and not yet entirely gone.

---
Some Dragonborn carry more than distant blood. In a rare few, a sleeping dragon soul lies coiled beneath the flesh, waiting for pressure, grief, or violence to awaken it. Others keep what remains of the old inheritance in another form: hidden relics, sealed treasures, and secret caches known as Hoard Threads, places where memory, wealth, and legacy were kept beyond the reach of divine greed.

---
Many Dragonborn do not walk the world in their true visage. They wear chosen faces, pass under borrowed shapes, and move through the ruins of Tyhra as if they were lesser things. Survival taught them that being overlooked is sometimes the highest form of power.

---
To play a Dragonborn is to bear greatness taught to hide from the world.
You are the remnant of something the gods tried to erase.
The question is not whether the old song still lives in you — but what will happen if it wakes.



---
:
| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Dragonborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 3 | 1-3 |

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The First Song

Dragonborn survived because they learned to disappear.

---
Yet once each day, at dawn or dusk, the Melody of the Beginning calls to them. It is not a song heard with the ears alone. It rises through blood, bone, breath, and memory, binding the last dragon-blooded souls across distance, grief, lineage, and hidden names.

---
For one brief moment, a Dragonborn may feel what was lost: kinship, ancestry, old wings, dead parents, forgotten cities, and the impossible warmth of belonging. Some hear only silence before the note. Some hear the voices of their line. Some feel the presence of those who came before them, not as ghosts, but as part of the same ancient soul-song.

---
This longing is dangerous. Dragonborn are taught to hide, to bury their names, to soften their presence, and to live as lesser things. But the First Song asks them to remember. It asks them to stop pretending. It asks them to answer.

---
A Dragonborn may resist the call, but never without ache. The danger is not only that enemies may hear the echo.

---
The danger is that, for one moment each day, the Dragonborn wants to be found.


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Unbound

The Wyld does not create species.

It creates responses.

The Unbound are those responses made flesh. They are not one people, one bloodline, or one culture. They are what happens when the world reacts to violation: when divine law is nailed into the land, when a sacred place is bent out of its nature, when hunger is manufactured, when Hell teaches the soil to rot, or when mortals are forced to become what they were never meant to be.

The Wyld responds.

But not every response wears the same shape.

Some responses become bodies of fang, horn, claw, feather, hunger, and sacred violence. These are the **Beasts**.

Some responses gather in circles, curses, roots, ash, old names, wet earth, and forbidden rites. These are the **Witches**.

Some responses remain almost human, but arm themselves with discipline, poisons, elixirs, relics, scars, and the knowledge of how to kill what should not be allowed to live. These are the **Banes**.

What binds all Unbound is not blood. It is opposition. They are made to resist what does not belong.

After the War of the Gods, when the Eight drove their order across the world and hammered Sentinels into the map like nails, many Unbound became hidden enemies of that sacred regime. They moved as saboteurs, curse-speakers, shrine-breakers, road-hunters, wilderness guardians, and cells of resistance born from forests, caverns, swamps, battlefields, and places where old wounds never closed.

Now the gods are gone, and Hell has taken their place.

The Unbound do not mourn the old order. To them, demons are only another invading pressure, another unnatural claim laid upon a wounded world. And so they fight again: against corruption, possession, false rule, branded flesh, poisoned land, and all who would teach the world to accept its own violation.

Most Unbound are not evil.

But neither are they gentle.

They are driven creatures of correction, instinct, rite, hunt, and unfinished purpose. In them, the self is often thinner than the response that made them. When they lose control, they do not simply become monsters.

They become what the Wyld made them to be.

To play an Unbound is to be the world’s response to something that should not exist.

You were not made for peace.

The question is whether you can still choose what must be broken.


| **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|
| Manifestation | Medium | 8 m |

---
##### Echo Burden: The Response Takes Over

All Unbound are responses of the Wyld.

They were shaped to oppose violation, corruption, false rule, divine control, demonic possession, and all things forced upon the world against its nature. But the response that made them is not passive. It waits beneath choice, beneath discipline, beneath identity.

When an Unbound is pushed too far, the response may take over.

This does not mean they become evil. It means they become dangerously pure. The Beast becomes the shape that hunts and tears. The Witch becomes the curse that speaks through flesh. The Bane becomes the weapon that can no longer stop hunting.

Every Unbound fears this moment, because it is powerful, useful, and sometimes necessary.

But it is never fully theirs.

---
::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### The Beasts

The Unbound are commonly divided into three broad responses: Beasts, Witches, and Banes. These are not nations or bloodlines. They are ways the Wyld answers violation.

Beasts are the body’s response.
They are the Unbound of transformation, instinct, animal memory, and sacred violence. Their power comes from the great shapes of the wild: wolf, bear, raven, stag, fox, boar, serpent, lynx, and stranger things remembered by old forests. The most famous Beasts are wolves, bears, and ravens, but they are not the only ones. Stags and foxes still appear in old tales, though they are rarer now, and some Beast-shapes are known only in specific regions or blood-soaked places.

:::
#### Predator Crown

Predator Crown is a Beast Unbound Echo Artifact representing the awakened hunting mind, bestial senses, fangs, beak, horns, jaws, and violent instinct of the Wyld written into the head.

It may manifest as wolfish jaws, bear teeth, raven eyes, antlers, fox ears, tusks, slit pupils, blackened gums, a second voice in the throat, or a skull-shape that becomes more visible when blood is near.

Choose one Predator Shape when this Artifact is created:

**Wolf, Bear, Raven, Stag, Fox, Boar, Serpent, Lynx, or another Beast shape approved by the GM.**

The chosen shape defines the appearance and narration of the Artifact, but all Predator Crowns use the same rules unless a specific entry says otherwise.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Bestial Head | Bite Damage / Predator Sense / Stone Power Support / Counter Damage / Damage Active Buff | Echo-bound |

Predator Crown occupies the Head Slot.

A Beast with Predator Crown cannot wear another Head Artifact, helmet, mask, crown, horn Artifact, magical headgear, or similar Head-based Artifact.

Predator Crown is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

#### Predator Crown Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Bite Damage | Base Value B: Predator Sense |
|---:|---:|---|
| Level 1 | 4d8 | - |
| Level 2 | 5d8 |- |
| Level 3 | 6d8 | - |
| Level 4 | 7d8 | Locate hostile intent within 16 m. |
| Level 5 | 8d8 | Locate hostile intent within 16 m. |
| Level 6 | 9d8 | Locate hostile intent within 16 m. |
| Level 7 | 10d8 | Identify hostile intent within 32 m. |
| Level 8 | 11d8 | Identify hostile intent within 32 m. |
| Level 9 | 12d8 | Identify hostile intent within 32 m. |
| Level 10 | 14d8, True Predator Crown | Target hostile intent within 64 m. |

Bite Damage is Natural Weapon Damage.

Bite Damage may represent a bite, beak strike, horn gore, tusk strike, throat snap, or similar head-based natural attack depending on the chosen Predator Shape.

Predator Sense follows the normal Predator Sense rules.

Predator Sense does not read thoughts, reveal full plans, or detect creatures with no hostile intent.

---
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Predator Stone Choice

When Predator Crown is created, choose one Predator Stone path.

This choice is permanent.

- **Might — Melee Damage**
- **Wits — Initiative Boost**
- **Intellect — Spell Raises**

Predator Crown supports the chosen Stone Power and pre-fills higher tiers as listed in the Level Progression.

The character must still pay all required lower tiers normally.

Although Head Artifacts normally follow Head-based Attribute access, Predator Crown is an Echo Artifact exception. Its Stones express the Beast’s hunting nature: raw force, killing precision, or the predatory mind reading the moment before violence.

---

### Witches 
Witches are the rite’s response.
They do not simply cast spells. They bind old agreements, speak curses, call roots through stone, rot purity, sour blood, twist luck, and remember names the temples tried to bury. Witch traditions often gather around a dominant working: Root Witches bind, entangle, hold, and awaken the deep green will of the land; Ruin Witches curse, break, hex, and turn certainty into collapse; Blight Witches poison, corrode, wither, and teach flesh, metal, and faith how to decay. A Witch does not force the Wyld. A Witch gives it a shape and pays the price for being heard.

#### Witch Staff

A Witch Staff is a Witch Unbound Echo Artifact representing the old rite made into wood, bone, root, ash, iron, thorn, rot, or blackened saint-wood.

Every Witch Staff is a two-handed arcane weapon and a Spell Focus from Level 1.

Witch Staffs are not one tradition. When this Artifact is created, choose one Witch Tradition:

- **Root Staff** — Root Witch
- **Ruin Staff** — Ruin Witch
- **Blight Staff** — Blight Witch

The chosen tradition determines the Staff’s Tradition Special, Main Spell, and Paired Spell.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Arcane Staff | Weapon Damage / Spell Focus / Tradition Special / Spell Raises Stone Power Support / Special Overdrive / Main Spell | Echo-bound |

Witch Staff occupies both hand Slots.

A Witch with Witch Staff cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time.

Witch Staff is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

Witch Staff counts as a **Spell Focus** from Level 1.

Witch Staff may be used with **Intellect**.

---

### Witch Staff Traditions

| Tradition | Staff Name | Tradition Special | Main Spell granted by Staff |
|---|---|---|---|
| Root Witch | Root Staff | Slow | Rootbinding | 
| Ruin Witch | Ruin Staff | Hex | Ruinous Word | 
| Blight Witch | Blight Staff | Corrode | Blightcall | 

The Main Spell is granted by the Staff through its Level Progression.

The Paired Spell is not granted by the Staff. A Witch character is expected to learn and advance the Paired Spell normally if they want to fully follow that tradition.

##### Root Staff

A Root Staff is grown from buried wood, grave roots, thorn knots, wet earth, and old circles drawn under the soil.


---

##### Ruin Staff

A Ruin Staff is made from blackened wood, cracked bone, broken shrine-metal, ash, old curses, and names that were erased from temple records.

---

##### Blight Staff

A Blight Staff is swollen with fungus, rust, sour water, corpse-bloom, mold, and green-black veins of living decay.

---
}}

### Banes 

Banes are Unbound who carry the hunt into the world.

They are not Beasts who surrender to animal shape, and they are not Witches who bind themselves primarily through covenants and rites.

Banes survive through preparation, endurance, old wrath, relics, tools, scars, and the refusal to die before monsters.

When a Bane Echo is created, choose one Bane Call:

- **Alchemist**
- **Greenwarden**
- **Relic Hunter**

The chosen Call defines the Bane's method, appearance, and expected Power direction.

It does not create a separate species.
It is the shape the hunt has taken.

---

#### Alchemist

Alchemists are Banes who survive through preparation.

They brew potions, oils, salves, bitter draughts, tinctures, and hunter mixtures from roots, ash, venom, blood, bone, and demon-tainted remains.

An Alchemist does not win by becoming a monster.

An Alchemist studies the monster, prepares the body, coats the blade, drinks the poison first, and walks into the dark with a plan.

Alchemists fit characters who want potions, oils, preparation, resistance, toxins, temporary empowerment, and controlled self-enhancement.

Their power is preparation, not transformation.

Idea: Artefact Body armor 2 Active Buffs which are used like potions and the vitality support for prolonging the active buffs

#### Alchemist Coat

The Alchemist Coat is a Bane Echo Artifact made from hardened leather, stitched iron, bone plates, sealed vials, black glass, monster sinew, silver hooks, blood tubes, hidden needles, and reinforced potion harnesses.

It is not a robe and not a trinket.

It is real armor.

The Alchemist Coat protects the body while carrying the volatile mixtures that allow a Bane Alchemist to survive the hunt.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Damage Potion / Extend Active Buff Stone Power Support / Critical Potion | Echo-bound |

Alchemist Coat occupies the Body Slot.

A Bane with Alchemist Coat cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time.

Alchemist Coat is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Medium Armor |
|---:|---:|
| Level 1 | 12 Armor |
| Level 2 | 13 Armor |
| Level 3 | 14 Armor |
| Level 4 | 15 Armor |
| Level 5 | 16 Armor |
| Level 6 | 17 Armor |
| Level 7 | 18 Armor |
| Level 8 | 19 Armor |
| Level 9 | 20 Armor |
| Level 10 | 22 Armor, True Alchemist Coat |

Alchemist Coat uses the normal Medium Armor drawbacks unless another rule explicitly changes them.

---

#### Alchemist Potions

Alchemist Potions are normal **Active Buffs**.

They follow all normal Active Buff rules.

A character may normally maintain only one Active Buff at a time.

If the Alchemist activates a new Potion while already maintaining another Active Buff, the previous Active Buff ends immediately unless another rule explicitly allows both to coexist.

The Potion name is only the Artifact's flavour name.

The mechanical Power is the listed existing Active Buff.

---

#### Red Potion

Red Potion floods the body with heat, rage, pressure, and violent focus.

Mechanically, Red Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Damage**.

---

#### Black Potion

Black Potion sharpens the senses until every pulse, blink, tendon, and weak point becomes visible.

Mechanically, Black Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Critical**.

---

#### Alchemist Stone

Alchemist Coat supports the **Vitality Ability: Extend Active Buff** Stone Power.

The Coat pre-fills higher tiers of that Stone Power through its Level Progression.

The character must still pay all required lower tiers normally.

Extend Active Buff only applies to one **Active Buff** you activate this turn.

It does not increase the Active Buff's Power Level.

It does not change the Buff's effect, value, range, AoE, target rules, or allowed effect structure.

It does not allow you to maintain an additional Active Buff.

It cannot extend Reactions, Movement Powers, Passives, Actives, Summons, Rituals, or ongoing Specials.

If multiple effects extend the same Active Buff, use only the highest duration increase unless a specific rule explicitly allows stacking.

---
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Greenwarden

Greenwardens are Banes touched by the old green wrath of the world.

They are inspired by ancient forest guardians, thorn-covered wanderers, antlered figures, bark-skinned protectors, moss-grown bodies, and the idea that nature itself remembers what was done to it.

A Greenwarden is not a Beast.

They do not become an animal.

They become something older: bark, thorn, moss, antler, sap, rot, root, and patient violence.

Greenwardens fit characters who want thorns, reflected harm, bark-skin, close-range punishment, defensive retaliation, regeneration, and the feeling that the world itself answers every wound.

Their power is endurance and retaliation.

---

#### Green Warden Mantle

The Green Warden Mantle is a Bane Echo Artifact grown from bark, thorn, moss, old roots, green iron, beast hide, sacred soil, and the last living strength of the wild places.

It is not a druidic robe and not a holy vestment.

It is the armor of a protector.

Green Wardens do not serve gods.

They stand between the living world and the things that would harvest it.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Thorns Active Buff / Ally Armor Reaction / Special Reduction Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Green Warden Mantle occupies the Body Slot.

A Green Warden with Green Warden Mantle cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time.

Green Warden Mantle is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Green Warden Mantle Base

| Level 2 | 13 Armor |
| Level 3 | 14 Armor |
| Level 4 | 15 Armor |
| Level 5 | 16 Armor |
| Level 6 | 17 Armor |
| Level 7 | 18 Armor |
| Level 8 | 19 Armor |
| Level 9 | 20 Armor |
| Level 10 | 22 Armor, True Green Warden Mantle |

Green Warden Mantle uses the normal Medium Armor drawbacks unless another rule explicitly changes them.

---

### Green Warden Passive Access

A Green Warden may choose the **Thorns Passive** during character creation.

Thorns Passive is exclusive to Green Wardens unless another rule explicitly grants access.

The Green Warden must still spend one Passive Slot to slot Thorns Passive.

If Thorns Passive is not slotted, it provides no benefit.

The **Thorns Active** is not exclusive. Any character may learn the Thorns Active if they meet its normal requirements.

A Green Warden may have both the Thorns Passive and the Thorns Active.

---

### Green Warden Thorns

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Thorns Active Buff**.

The Thorns Active Buff represents thorns, roots, bark splinters, hooked vines, green iron spines, or living brambles striking back at nearby enemies.

The name is the Artifact's flavour name.

The mechanical Power is the existing **Thorns Active Buff**.

---

### Green Warden Protection

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Reaction: Ally Armor**.

This Reaction represents roots rising, bark hardening, thorns catching a blow, or the Warden forcing the living world between an ally and harm.

The Reaction follows all normal Reaction rules.

It applies only to the triggering ally hit or damage instance.

It does not create a maintained state.

It is not an Active Buff.

---

### Green Warden Stone

Green Warden Mantle supports the **Resolve Ability: Special Reduction** Stone Power.

The Mantle pre-fills higher tiers of that Stone Power through its Level Progression.

The character must still pay all required lower tiers normally.

---

### Thorns I-III

The Warden's body becomes hostile ground.

Roots tighten.

Bark cracks open.

Thorns push through armor seams.

The world itself remembers how to hurt those who come too close.

Mechanically, Thorns I-III use the normal **Thorns Active Buff** at the listed Power Level.

---

### Warden's Guard I-III

The Green Warden does not protect through command or divine light.

They protect through bark, root, thorn, shielded instinct, and the refusal of the living world to let another body break.

Mechanically, Warden's Guard I-III use the normal **Reaction: Ally Armor** at the listed Power Level.

---

Green Warden Mantle is tied to the Bane Burden: **The Hollow Hunt**.

When the Green Warden loses control, they no longer protect only what should be protected.

The forest answers through them.

{{wide
#### Hunter's Scourge

Hunter's Scourge is a Demon Hunter Echo Artifact: a chain-whip, iron lash, hooked cord, weighted scourge, relic whip, or segmented killing chain built to drag monsters into reach and destroy them.

Demon Hunters are not holy knights.

They are trained killers of demons, undead, fiends, cursed things, and monsters that survived too long.

Where Alchemists survive the hunt by changing their own bodies, and Green Wardens protect what still lives, Demon Hunters end the hunt by pulling the monster close and burning it out of the world.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Chain Whip | Whip Damage / Finesse / Reach / Smite Lash / Snap Chain / Whirling Scourge | Echo-bound |

Hunter's Scourge occupies both hand Slots.

A Demon Hunter with Hunter's Scourge cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time.

Hunter's Scourge is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Hunter's Scourge Base

Hunter's Scourge uses the normal **Whip** as its Base Weapon.

| Base Weapon | Damage | Hands | Specials |

| Whip | 4d8 | 1 | Finesse, Reach (+1 m) |

It keeps **Finesse** and **Reach (+1 m)** from the Base Weapon.

Choose or define one final Scourge Damage, Reach, Smite Lash, Snap Chain, Whirling Scourge, demon-hunting, undead-hunting, chain, whip, relic, or Demon Hunter effect with GM approval. | True Hunter's Scourge |

---

### Smite Lash I-III

The Demon Hunter strikes one supernatural enemy with the full weight of the hunt.

The chain burns, cracks, hooks, tears, or flashes with old killing rites.

Mechanically, Smite Lash I-III use the normal **Melee Smite Attack** at the listed Power Level.

---

### Snap Chain I-III

The Demon Hunter refuses to let the monster choose the distance.

When prey crosses the Hunter's Threat Zone, the chain snaps out and drags it back into the killing line.

Mechanically, Snap Chain I-III use the normal **Reaction: Counter Damage + Pull** at the listed Power Level.

---

### Whirling Scourge I-III

The Demon Hunter turns the chain into a circle of judgment.

Hooks, iron links, silvered edges, weighted heads, and relic knots carve through everything close enough to be caught in the spin.

Mechanically, Whirling Scourge I-III use the normal **Melee AoE Smite Attack** at the listed Power Level.

---

Hunter's Scourge is tied to the Bane Burden: **The Hollow Hunt**.

When the Demon Hunter loses control, the chain no longer waits for monsters.

It only looks for prey.


}}

## Deathless - WIP

In the lands of Tyra, death does not always hold. Not everywhere. Not quickly. Not all at once. But slowly, quietly, and without mercy, something has begun to loosen.

A corpse opens its eyes beneath wet soil. A drowned soldier coughs river mud from dead lungs. A burned woman stands among ashes and no longer feels the fire. A child hears someone crying from inside a sealed grave.

Most people call them cursed. Some call them monsters. Some call them omens of the end. The Deathless themselves rarely know more than anyone else. They know that healing no longer answers them. They know that pain is distant, strange, or delayed. They know that life shines in ways it never did before. And they know the call.

A distant pull beneath thought. A hollow command without words. A road inside the skull. The call of the **Hollow King**.

Some Deathless can ignore it. Some can resist it for years. Some wake and begin walking before they even remember their own name. They do not know what waits at the end of that call. They only know that something dead remembers them.

---

### Hollow Crown

The Hollow Crown is the Echo of the Deathless. It is not always a visible crown. It may appear as a black iron circlet, a cracked funeral mask, a bone halo, a rusted helm, a ring of ash beneath the skin, a crown-shaped scar, a cold mark upon the brow, or an unseen weight around the skull.

It is the place where death failed to finish its work.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Deathless Echo | Deathless Body / Life Sight / Hollow Stillness / Life Absorption | Echo-bound |

Hollow Crown occupies the Head Slot. A Deathless character with Hollow Crown cannot use another Head Artifact, magical helm, crown, mask, circlet, or head-based Echo Artifact at the same time. Hollow Crown is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Deathless Nature

Deathless are not automatically evil. They are not servants of demons by nature. They do not hate the living simply because they no longer live. But death changes what a body needs, what pain means, and how long a soul can remain whole.

A Deathless character does not heal like the living. They cannot restore Health through natural rest, normal healing, magical healing, healing potions, regeneration, or standard healing Powers unless a rule explicitly states that it works on Deathless characters.

Instead, Deathless restore themselves through **Life Absorption**.

---

### Life Sight

Life Sight is a Combat Sense granted by Hollow Crown. A Deathless can sense living creatures as warmth, pulse, breath, blood, hunger, pressure, or flickering life-force.

Life Sight does not reveal thoughts, emotions, memories, names, loyalties, lies, intentions, or moral alignment. Life Sight does not detect objects, constructs, illusions without life, corpses without living force, or other Deathless unless another rule says otherwise. Life Sight is blocked by effects that block Combat Senses, supernatural concealment, or GM-approved barriers.

| Hollow Crown Level | Life Sight Range |
|---:|---:|
| Level 1 | 8 m |
| Level 4 | 16 m |
| Level 7 | 32 m |
| Level 10 | 64 m |

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Deathless Body

Deathless Body is an exclusive Passive granted by Hollow Crown. It cannot be learned normally. It cannot be selected by living characters.

Deathless Body changes how Health Penalties affect the character. The Deathless body does not react to injury like living flesh. Pain arrives late. Blood no longer matters in the same way. Broken movement, torn muscle, shock, and exhaustion lose their hold step by step.

Deathless characters still lose Health normally. Deathless characters can still be Incapacitated normally. Deathless Body only changes the penalties caused by damaged Health Levels.

---

### Deathless Body Progression

At Level 1, Hollow Crown grants **Deathless Body I**. The first two Health Penalty steps are reduced by two steps.

| Normal Health Penalty | Deathless Body I Penalty |
|---:|---:|
| 0% | 0% |
| 10% | 0% |
| 20% | 0% |
| 30% | 10% |
| 40% | 20% |

A Deathless character with Deathless Body I can never suffer more than **20% Health Penalty** from normal Health Penalty steps.

At higher levels, Hollow Crown improves Deathless Body.

| Hollow Crown Level | Deathless Body Effect |
|---:|---|
| Level 1 | Deathless Body I. Health Penalties are shifted down by 2 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 20%. |
| Level 4 | Deathless Body II. Health Penalties are shifted down by 3 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 10%. |
| Level 7 | Deathless Body III. Health Penalties are shifted down by 4 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 0%. |
| Level 10 | True Deathless Body. You suffer no normal Health Penalties and remain functional until Incapacitated. |

Deathless Body does not prevent special penalties from Conditions, Specials, curses, magic, control effects, exhaustion effects, or other rules unless those penalties are explicitly Health Penalties.

---

### Life Absorption

Deathless cannot restore Health through normal healing. They restore themselves by absorbing life.

Life Absorption is not a combat action. It cannot be used during combat unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

Life Absorption requires a helpless, willing, dying, freshly dead, sacrificed, ritually prepared, or GM-approved living creature. Life Absorption is not automatically evil, but it is never harmless.

The exact method depends on the Deathless. Some drink blood. Some steal breath. Some consume warmth. Some draw the final heartbeat from a dying body. Some kneel in silence beside the dead until the corpse becomes cold and the Deathless becomes whole.

| Hollow Crown Level | Life Absorption |
|---:|---|
| Level 2 | Life Absorption I. Restore up to 1 Health Level through a completed Life Absorption. |
| Level 5 | Life Absorption II. Restore up to 2 Health Levels through a completed Life Absorption. |
| Level 8 | Life Absorption III. Restore up to 3 Health Levels through a completed Life Absorption. |
| Level 10 | True Life Absorption. Restore up to full Health through a completed Life Absorption if the sacrifice, source, or ritual is sufficient. |

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

---

### Hollow Stillness

Hollow Stillness represents the cold distance between the Deathless and the needs of living flesh. Fear becomes quieter. Pain becomes slower. Poison finds less warmth. Disease finds less purchase. The body is still vulnerable, but it no longer belongs completely to life.

| Hollow Crown Level | Hollow Stillness |
|---:|---|
| Level 3 | Hollow Stillness I. Gain a minor bonus or advantage against Fear, Pain, Poison, Disease, Suffocation, Exhaustion, and effects that rely on living flesh, as approved by the GM. |
| Level 6 | Hollow Stillness II. Improve the bonus or advantage from Hollow Stillness I. |
| Level 9 | Hollow Stillness III. Further improve the bonus or advantage from Hollow Stillness I. |
| Level 10 | True Hollow Stillness. The GM may define one final resistance, immunity, or deathless protection appropriate to the character. |

Hollow Stillness does not grant blanket immunity unless a specific rule says so. It does not protect against all magic. It does not protect against all mind effects. It does not protect against all divine, demonic, or soul-based effects.

---

### Level Progression

| **Echo Level** | **Name** | **Type** | **Range** | **AoE** | **Duration** | **Effect** | **Special** |
|:--:|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--|:--|
| **1** | **Deathless Body I** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Gain the exclusive **Deathless Body** Passive. Health Penalties are shifted down by 2 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 20%. Gain **Life Sight 8 m**. | Deathless Body |
| **2** | **Life Absorption I** | Ritual / Echo Function | Touch | — | Special | You may restore up to **1 Health Level** through completed Life Absorption. You cannot heal through normal healing unless a rule explicitly works on Deathless characters. | Life Absorption |
| **3** | **Hollow Stillness I** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Gain a minor bonus or advantage against Fear, Pain, Poison, Disease, Suffocation, Exhaustion, and effects that rely on living flesh, as approved by the GM. | Hollow Stillness |
| **4** | **Deathless Body II** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Health Penalties are shifted down by 3 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 10%. Life Sight increases to **16 m**. | Deathless Body |
| **5** | **Life Absorption II** | Ritual / Echo Function | Touch | — | Special | You may restore up to **2 Health Levels** through completed Life Absorption. | Life Absorption |
| **6** | **Hollow Stillness II** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Improve the bonus or advantage from Hollow Stillness I. | Hollow Stillness |
| **7** | **Deathless Body III** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Health Penalties are shifted down by 4 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 0%. Life Sight increases to **32 m**. | Deathless Body |
| **8** | **Life Absorption III** | Ritual / Echo Function | Touch | — | Special | You may restore up to **3 Health Levels** through completed Life Absorption. | Life Absorption |
| **9** | **Hollow Stillness III** | Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Further improve the bonus or advantage from Hollow Stillness I. | Hollow Stillness |
| **10** | **True Hollow Crown** | Ultimate | Self | — | Permanent | You suffer no normal Health Penalties and remain functional until Incapacitated. Life Sight increases to **64 m**. You may restore up to full Health through Life Absorption if the source or ritual is sufficient. Define one final Hollow Crown, Deathless Body, Life Sight, Life Absorption, Hollow Stillness, memory, hunger, crown, grave, or dead-road effect with GM approval. | True Hollow Crown |

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### The Call

All Deathless hear the call. It is not always a voice. Sometimes it is a road seen in dreams. Sometimes it is a pressure behind the eyes. Sometimes it is the taste of old ash. Sometimes it is a name they know without remembering when they learned it.

The call belongs to the **Hollow King**. Nothing more is certain.

Some Deathless resist the call. Some follow it. Some fear it. Some believe it is the only thing keeping them from becoming truly empty. No Deathless begins with certainty about what waits at the end.

}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Social Combat — Phased Challenge

> Social Combat is a structured challenge for important conversations, negotiations, intrigues, interrogations, trials, seductions, public debates, and other high-pressure social scenes.

Social Combat works like a **Boss Encounter**.

The players define a goal.  
The GM divides the scene into **3 or more phases**.  
Each phase begins with roleplay and ends with **one Lead roll**.

Only the **final phase** decides the main outcome.  
Earlier phases create **Setup** or **Leverage** that makes the final phase stronger.

---

### Echo Cards in Social Combat

Echo Cards are especially useful in Social Combat.

Many Echo Cards give a character a strong reason to use Skills such as:

- **Persuasion**
- **Deception**
- **Etiquette**
- **Empathy**
- **Intimidation**
- **Leadership**
- **Negotiation**
- **Performance**
- **Seduction**
- **Streetwise**

When an Echo Card fits the current phase, it can shape how the character contributes to the scene.

A character might use an Echo Card to read a room, pressure a rival, hide their intent, win trust, expose a weakness, or support the Lead Actor.

---

### 1) Encounter Setup

The players define their goal.

Examples:

- win a noble’s support
- extract a secret
- ruin someone’s reputation
- calm an angry crowd
- expose a liar
- negotiate safe passage
- turn enemies against each other

The GM defines:

- **Phases:** usually 3, but larger scenes may use more
- **Allowed Skills:** which Skills fit each phase
- **Base TN:** usually **16**, adjusted by the scene
- **Boss Defenses:** usually one or more social TNs
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
Common Boss Defenses:

| **Defense** | **Used For** |
|:--|:--|
| **Influence TN** | charm, status, public pressure, social positioning |
| **Resolve TN** | resistance, intimidation, seduction, blackmail, emotional pressure |
| **Wits TN** | reading the room, noticing shifts, catching openings |
| **Intellect TN** | arguments, evidence, legal structures, formal reasoning |

---

### 2) Phase Loop

Each phase follows this structure:

1. **Roleplay the phase**
2. Choose the **Lead Actor**
3. Choose fitting **Support actions**
4. Resolve Support rolls
5. The Lead makes **one roll**
6. Count the Raises gained

The Lead may declare Raises before rolling.  
Each declared Raise increases the TN by **+4**.

If the Lead succeeds, the declared Raises are gained.  
If the Lead fails, the phase fails forward and creates Stress.

---

### 3) Lead & Support

**Lead Actor:**  
One character leads the phase and makes the main roll.

**Support:**  
Each other participating PC may support **once per phase**.

A Support roll uses a fitting Skill against the phase TN.

- **On success:** add the supporter’s **Mastery Rank** as a flat bonus to the Lead roll.
- **On failure:** no bonus is gained. The GM may add pressure, Heat, or a complication.

Support must be justified through roleplay.

---

### 4) Setup Pool

Only the early phases create Setup.

For a standard 3-phase Social Combat:

- **Phase 1** creates Setup
- **Phase 2** creates Setup
- **Phase 3** is the Final Phase

Setup Gain:

| **Raises gained in phase** | **Setup Gain** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **0 Raises** | **−1** |
| **1 Raise** | **0** |
| **2 Raises** | **+1** |
| **3 Raises** | **+2** |
| **4 Raises** | **+3** |
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
Formula:

`Setup Gain = Raises − 1`

The Setup Pool is the sum of Setup gained before the Final Phase.

---

### 5) Final Phase

In the Final Phase, make the final Lead roll.

Then calculate:

`Final Total Raises = Raises from Final Phase + Setup Pool`

Compare the result to the encounter threshold.

| **Final Total Raises** | **Outcome** |
|:--:|:--|
| **Less than 4** | Fail. The plan collapses, turns against the party, or succeeds only at a severe cost. |
| **4–7** | Success. The party achieves the goal. |
| **8+** | Overdeliver. The party achieves the goal and gains an additional advantage, secret, ally, leverage, or cleaner outcome. |

---

### 6) Stress

If a phase roll fails, the story continues.

However, the participating characters suffer:

`4d8 Stress per failed phase`

This Stress may be split freely among the participating PCs.

The GM should describe this Stress as social pressure, shame, fear, doubt, emotional exhaustion, panic, spiritual strain, or the cost of maintaining the act.

---

### Quick Reference

- Social Combat is used for important social scenes.
- The players define the goal.
- The GM divides the encounter into **3 or more phases**.
- Each phase has roleplay first, then **one Lead roll**.
- Each other PC may support **once per phase**.
- Successful Support adds the supporter’s **MR** to the Lead roll.
- Raises in early phases create **Setup**.
- Final outcome is decided by:

`Final Raises + Setup Pool`

- Default outcome:
  - **<4** Fail
  - **4–7** Success
  - **8+** Overdeliver
- Failed phases cause **4d8 Stress**, split among participating PCs.
  
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Disadvantages

---
:


Every hero carries scars — some visible, others etched into the soul.

Disadvantages are flaws, burdens, weaknesses, obligations, fears, curses, enemies, dependencies, or restrictions that make your character more complicated and more dangerous to play.

They are not meant to make a character unplayable.  
They are meant to create pressure, hard choices, complications, and drama.

---

### Open List Rule

The Disadvantages in this chapter are **examples and templates**.

They are not a closed list.

Players may create custom Disadvantages with GM approval.

When creating a custom Disadvantage, define:

- what triggers it,
- how often it matters,
- what happens when it matters,
- whether the character can resist it,
- and how many Disadvantage Points it is worth.

A Disadvantage should be clear enough that both player and GM understand when it applies.

---

### Disadvantage Rules

- You may pick Disadvantages up to a maximum of **8 Disadvantage Points** total.
- Your maximum **Disadvantage Points** become your maximum **Reroll Points**.
- After each **Safe Haven Rest**, you start at your maximum Reroll Points.

If a Disadvantage calls for a control roll, use:

`Resolve k1`

No Raises may be declared on this roll.

---

### Rewarding Disadvantages

When a player actively roleplays a Disadvantage in a way that creates meaningful tension, risk, drama, sacrifice, or complication, the GM may allow that character to recover **1 Reroll Point**.

This should reward real play, not empty mention.

Good examples include:

- acting on a fear even when it creates danger,
- refusing an easy solution because of an oath,
- accepting a complication because of a flaw,
- exposing a weakness at a dramatic moment,
- following an addiction, curse, obsession, or trauma when it matters,
- allowing Hunted, Unluck, or a Physical Scar to shape the scene,
- choosing a harder path because the Disadvantage genuinely matters.

A player cannot recover more Reroll Points than their maximum.

The GM decides when a Disadvantage was played strongly enough to restore a Reroll Point.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Addiction *(2 Points)*

You are dependent on a physical, mental, magical, spiritual, or metaphysical substance, ritual, state, or contact to remain stable.

This may be addiction, withdrawal, dependency, compulsion, or supernatural need.

**Effect:**

If you are denied the source of your Addiction:

| **Time Without** | **Penalty** |
|---|---:|
| 1 day | –2d8 to all Skill Dice Pools |
| 1 week | –4d8 to all Skill Dice Pools |
| 1 month | –8d8 to all Skill Dice Pools |
| 3 months or more | –16d8 to all Skill Dice Pools |

The GM may also call for a `Resolve k1` roll when the Addiction is offered, threatened, denied, or becomes dangerous to pursue.

On failure, the character suffers an appropriate complication, such as distraction, withdrawal, risky behavior, accepting a bad bargain, or exposing their weakness.

**Examples:**

- A Sentinel requires sacred oil to regulate its systems.
- A magic user needs Dreamdust to silence voices.
- A believer is dependent on divine communion and suffers agony without prayer.
- A blood-bound warrior craves battle or bloodshed.
- A cursed oracle needs visions, dreams, or forbidden contact to remain stable.

---

### Berserker’s Curse *(2 Points)*

You lose control when badly wounded, succumbing to blood-fury.

**Trigger:**  
When you take **Wounds ≥ your Vitality**, make a `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 8**.

No Raises may be declared on this roll.

On failure, you go **Berserk** for `1d8 / 2 rounds`.

**While Berserk:**

- You gain **+1d8 damage per Mastery Rank**.
- You must attack the nearest creature, friend or foe.
- At the end of each round, repeat the `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 8** to regain control.

If you succeed, Berserk ends.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Hunted *(1–3 Points)*

Someone or something is hunting you and it will not stop.

This may be a person, monster, faction, noble house, guild, cult, spirit, divine agent, demonic patron, family, government, or supernatural force.

The examples below are not a closed list.

| **Points** | **Threat** | **Examples** |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | A limited or personal threat | A bounty hunter, rival, debt collector, betrayed lover, local guard captain |
| 2 | An organized threat with resources | A cult, noble house, mercenary guild, criminal syndicate, church order |
| 3 | A powerful or far-reaching threat | A demon lord, archwitch, archangel, ancient spirit, royal inquisition, planar hunter |

**Define:**

- who is hunting you,
- why they are hunting you,
- how much they know,
- how far their reach extends,
- whether they want capture, revenge, punishment, service, silence, or death.

**Effect:**  
The GM may introduce chases, clues, agents, confrontations, traps, rumors, or social complications connected to the hunter.

Hunted should create story pressure.  
It should not appear randomly just to punish the player.

---
:

### Physical Scars *(1–3 Points)*

Your body bears lasting damage, weakness, mutation, instability, or limitation.

Physical Scars are not limited to the examples below.

They may affect:

- sight,
- hearing,
- sleep,
- movement,
- hands,
- breathing,
- stamina,
- pain,
- balance,
- coordination,
- appearance,
- or physical reliability.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
| **Example Flaw** | **Points** | **Effect** |
|---|---:|---|
| One-Eyed | 1 | –1k0 to Ranged Attacks and Perception checks based on sight. |
| One-Handed | 2 | You cannot dual wield or use a shield and one-handed weapon at the same time. |
| Heavy Sleeper | 1 | You do not wake from normal noise. You wake only when damaged, physically shaken, or directly disturbed. |
| Light Sleeper | 1 | You sleep poorly and may suffer complications during rests, watches, or exhaustion scenes. |
| Sleepless | 2–3 | You cannot sleep normally. Define with the GM how you recover during rests. |
| Chronic Pain | 1–2 | Under stress, pain may impose penalties or require a `Resolve k1` roll to ignore. |
| Damaged Lungs | 1–2 | Smoke, poison gas, drowning, long chases, or exhaustion affect you more severely. |
| Trembling Hands | 1–2 | Fine manipulation, delicate tools, ranged precision, or spell focus may become harder under pressure. |
| Fragile Frame | 3 | Your Health Track has one fewer box on every level. |

The important question is not whether the flaw appears on this list.

The important question is:

> **How does this complicate play, and how often does it matter?**

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mental Restrictions *(1–3 Points)*

You are bound by your past, beliefs, trauma, instincts, compulsions, vows, fears, or personality.

Mental Restrictions are not ordinary preferences.

They must be strong enough to create real pressure in play.

The examples below are not a closed list.

---

#### Oaths

- No killing
- Chivalric code
- Always keep promises
- Never abandon an ally
- Never refuse a duel
- Never lie
- Never harm the helpless
- Never break hospitality
- Always repay debts

---

#### Fears

Fears should be handled like psychological limitations.

A Fear must have a clear trigger and a clear consequence.

Examples:

- Claustrophobia
- Fear of fire
- Fear of deep water
- Fear of darkness
- Fear of being restrained
- Fear of undead
- Fear of demons
- Fear of divine symbols
- Fear of mirrors
- Fear of losing control
- Fear of becoming a monster
- Paranoia
- Hatred for a group

---

#### Personality Traits

- Arrogant — always needs to prove superiority
- Coward — retreats or hesitates when seriously threatened
- Vengeful — cannot forgive a serious offense
- Gullible — easily moved by sad stories, pleading, or false innocence
- In love with someone — acts irrationally when that person is in danger
- Obsessive — cannot ignore a specific goal, clue, person, or mystery
- Proud — cannot accept humiliation
- Cruel — struggles to show mercy
- Merciful — struggles to finish helpless enemies

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mental Restriction Rule

To act **against** your Mental Restriction, make a `Resolve k1` roll.

No Raises may be declared on this roll.

| **Difficulty** | **TN** | **Meaning** | **Points** |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| Easy | 6 | Minor resistance or mild discomfort | 1 |
| Normal | 10 | Strong internal conflict | 2 |
| Hard | 14 | Violates a core belief, fear, vow, or identity | 3 |

On success, you may act against the Restriction for the moment.

On failure, the Restriction creates a complication.  
This may mean hesitation, refusal, panic, retreat, escalation, social conflict, tactical risk, or another consequence agreed by the GM and player.

A Mental Restriction should create drama and pressure.  
It should not remove the character from play every time it appears.

---

### Unluck *(1–3 Points)*

You are cursed with misfortune.

At the start of each session, the GM gains Misfortune Tokens based on your Unluck value.

The GM may spend a Misfortune Token to:

- worsen the outcome of one of your failed rolls,
- introduce an unlikely narrative obstacle,
- create an inconvenient complication,
- affect an ally caught in your bad luck,
- or make an already bad situation worse.

| **Points** | **Misfortune Tokens per Session** |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | `1d8 / 2` |
| 2 | `1d8` |
| 3 | `2d8` |

Misfortune should complicate the story.  
It should not be used to invalidate player choices or turn success into failure.

---


### Vulnerability *(1-4 Points)*

You are unusually susceptible to a specific harmful Special.

Choose one eligible negative value-based Special.

Whenever that Special is applied to you, increase its applied value by your **Vulnerability value**.

This only affects the Special when it is applied.

It does not trigger again when the Special ticks.  
It does not add extra hits, extra stacks, or a separate Special.  
It does not make the Special harder to remove unless a rule explicitly says so.

**Example:**  
If you have **Vulnerability: Ruin(2)** and an enemy applies **Ruin(3)** to you, you suffer **Ruin(5)** instead.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Eligible Specials:**

| **Special** | **Vulnerability Effect** |
|---|---|
| **Lacerate(X)** | When Lacerate is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Ruin(X)** | When Ruin is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Slow(X)** | When Slow is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Hex(X)** | When Hex is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |

---

### Reroll Points — Fractures of Faith

Reroll Points represent the shattered, fragmented nature of the characters.

Their memories, weaknesses, scars, and nightmares are not only burdens.  
They can also become a source of desperate strength.

---

#### Starting and Recovery

- Your maximum **Reroll Points** equal your total **Disadvantage Points**.
- You recover all Reroll Points after a **Safe Haven Rest**.
- You cannot have more Reroll Points than your maximum.
- The GM may allow you to recover **1 Reroll Point** when you strongly roleplay a Disadvantage in a way that creates meaningful tension, risk, sacrifice, or complication.

This reward should be given for real consequences, not for simply mentioning the flaw.

---

#### Using Reroll Points

You may spend **1 Reroll Point** to reroll all dice of one roll you make.

You must keep the new result.

You may also spend **1 Reroll Point** to force the GM to reroll all dice of one roll they make.

The GM must keep the new result.

Reroll Points may be combined with other reroll effects unless a rule says otherwise.

> *You hesitate. Voices in your head. And yet — you try again.*

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### Example: Disadvantages

The following examples show how a character might combine Disadvantages up to the maximum of **8 Disadvantage Points**.

These are not fixed packages.  
They only show how Disadvantages can create pressure, story hooks, and Reroll Points.

---
:
#### Example: Marked Fugitive

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Hunted** *(Demonic Cult)* | **3** | The GM may introduce cult agents, informants, ambushes, or social pressure connected to the hunter. | Someone wants the character captured, silenced, or returned alive. |
| **Addiction** *(Ritual Incense)* | **2** | If denied the source, the character suffers the normal Addiction penalties over time. | The character needs a nightly rite to remain stable. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Never Trust the Faithful of the Old Gods)* | **2** | Acting against this restriction requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | Old betrayal makes trust in believers, temple servants, or keepers of the old rites difficult. |
| **Unluck** | **1** | The GM gains Misfortune Tokens according to the Unluck rules. | Trouble finds the character at the worst possible time. |

**Total:** **8 Points**

---
:
#### Example: Broken Veteran

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Physical Scar** *(Damaged Lungs)* | **2** | Smoke, poison gas, drowning, long chases, or exhaustion may affect the character more severely. | The war left wounds that never fully healed. |
| **Hunted** *(Old War Enemy)* | **2** | The GM may introduce pursuers, bounty notices, old rivals, or dangerous recognition. | Someone from the past still wants revenge. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Never Abandon an Ally)* | **2** | Leaving an ally behind requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | Loyalty is strength, but also a chain. |
| **Berserker’s Curse** | **2** | When badly wounded, the character risks losing control according to the Berserker’s Curse rules. | Pain turns discipline into fury. |

**Total:** **8 Points**

---
:
#### Example: Cursed Wanderer

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Vulnerability** *(Ruin)* | **4** | Whenever the character is affected by **Ruin(X)**, they suffer the doubled effect listed under Vulnerability. | Fire does not merely burn them; it awakens the curse. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Fear of Fire)* | **2** | Acting calmly near dangerous flame requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | The character remembers burning, even when no fire is present. |
| **Physical Scar** *(Trembling Hands)* | **1** | Fine manipulation, delicate tools, ranged precision, or spell focus may become harder under pressure. | The body still shakes from an old trauma. |
| **Unluck** | **1** | The GM gains Misfortune Tokens according to the Unluck rules. | Misfortune follows like a shadow. |

**Total:** **8 Points**


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Disadvantages

Alaris takes the full **8 Disadvantage Points**. Those points become her maximum Reroll Points, but every choice also creates a real complication.

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **How it matters** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Lost Love: Liora** | **3** | Her connection to Liora can override caution or immediate priorities. |
| **Light Sleeper** | **2** | Rest becomes fragile in unsafe or unfamiliar places. |
| **Nightmares** | **1** | Sleep may return her to memories, omens, or images she cannot control. |
| **Vegetarian** | **2** | Scarcity can turn a personal conviction into a survival problem. |

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Disadvantages.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

{{note
##### What this example shows
A useful Disadvantage creates decisions and story pressure. It should not exist only to generate Reroll Points.
}}

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## The Finished Character: Alaris

The final character should read as one connected plan rather than a collection of unrelated high values.

| **Final element** | **Alaris** |
|:--|:--|
| **Mastery Rank** | 2 |
| **Core role** | Mobile melee skirmisher |
| **Total Evade** | 17 from the MR baseline and her artifacts |
| **Armor** | 2 from Mastery Rank soak |
| **Health per Bar** | 16 from Vitality 8 x 2 |
| **Stress capacity** | 6 from Resolve 4 + Intellect 2 |
| **Main weapon** | Moonlight Greatsword: 5d8, Finesse, Precision(2) |
| **Awakened Stones** | Agility 1 and Vitality 1 |

### Her combat rhythm

1. Reach a useful melee position through normal movement, Bounding Leap, or an Agility Stone option.
2. Choose between direct damage and applying Sundered for the group.
3. Let high Evade answer ordinary direct attacks.
4. Reserve Phasing for the most dangerous hits.
5. Spend Stones when movement, survival, or Special removal cannot wait.

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Finished.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

{{note
##### What the complete example teaches
A strong Mastery character does not need to cover every role. Alaris has a clear offensive purpose, a clear defensive plan, and several supporting choices that express the same identity.
}}

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Stone Abilities

Stones are a player-facing measure of your awakened potential. In the fiction, they represent inner strength, not physical objects.
A warrior who has awakened Might unleashes raw destruction; a rogue who has awakened Agility moves like a blur of shadows; a mage who has awakened Intellect overloads the arcane weave.

---

Once an Attribute reaches **8**, its true potential awakens:
you gain a **Stone Pool** for that Attribute and unlock its **Stone Powers**.  

---
:
### The Core Chain
- **Whenever an Attribute reaches 8 or any multiple of 8** (**8, 16, 24, 32, 40…**) → gain **+1 Stone** in that Attribute’s Stone Pool.
- That Attribute unlocks **8 Stone Powers**:
  - **4 Generic Stone Powers** (same for all Attributes)
  - **4 Attribute Stone Powers** (unique to that Attribute)
- **Each Stone = 1 Activation**:  
  Each round, you may activate Stone Powers up to the number of **Ready Stones** you have for that Attribute.
- **Every additional +8 Attribute** (16, 24, 32, …) → gain **+1 Stone**, which means **+1 Activation** per round.

> In short: **more Attribute → more Stones → more activations**, while the **8 Stone Powers stay the same**.

---

#### Stone States

Stones exist in 4 states:
- **Ready**: available to be used.
- **Exhausted**: spent during the current combat flow. Exhausted Stones can return through **regeneration**.
- **Sealed**: locked by Rituals; cannot be regenerated at end of round. It returns only after a **Safe Haven Rest**.
- **Bound**: Permanently invested (Artifacts, Familiars, Summons, etc.). Bound Stones are not part of your usable pool and do not regenerate. They return to your Stone Pool only when the bond is **broken** or the investment is **released**.

---

#### Spending Stones
- On your turn, you may spend any number of **Ready Stones** (up to your pool).
- Each Stone spent grants **1 Activation** of a Stone Power and becomes **Exhausted**.

**Exponential cost progression per Stone Power (per turn):**

| Usage of the same Stone Power | Cost |
|-------------------------------|------|
| 1st use                       | 1 Stone |
| 2nd use                       | 2 Stones |
| 3rd use                       | 4 Stones |
| 4th use                       | 8 Stones |
| etc.                          | ×2 per additional use |

---

Default: doubling costs reset at the start of your turn.  
If a Stone Power states “cumulative per combat”, the doubling cost persists until combat ends.

---
:
#### Regeneration
- At the end of each round, you regenerate a total number of Exhausted Stones equal to your Mastery Rank, chosen from any of your Attribute Stone Pools.
- Sealed / Bound Stones do not return by you Regeneration.



---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

| **Extra Movement** | Gain **1 additional Movement** this round. | Gain **2 additional Movements** this round. | Gain **3 additional Movements** this round. | Gain **4 additional Movements** this round. |
| **Extra Reaction** | Gain **1 additional Reaction** this round. | Gain **2 additional Reactions** this round. | Gain **3 additional Reactions** this round. | Gain **4 additional Reactions** this round. |
| **Exchange Passive** | Swap **1 active Passive** with another Passive you know. | Swap **1 active Passive** with another Passive you know. | Swap **2 active Passives** with other Passives you know. | Swap **2 active Passives** with other Passives you know. |

::::

### Last Breath

Once per Safe Haven Rest, when you would drop to Incapacitated, you may burn **1 Stone** from any Attribute.

---
You remain conscious with **1 free box** in your Wounded Health Bar.

---
Any remaining damage from the triggering damage instance is ignored.

---
You cannot use Last Breath again until you complete a Safe Haven Rest.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Types of Powers

Powers are divided into five main types:

| **Power Type** | **Short Description** |
|---|---|
| **Active** | Action-based Powers for attacks, healing, effects, zones, barriers, and Special delivery. |
| **Passive** | Ongoing character features that define your baseline and combat identity. |
| **Active Buff** | Temporary combat states, stances, auras, transformations, or short-term enhancements. |
| **Reaction** | Immediate responses to specific triggers outside your turn. |
| **Movement Power** | Special movement options that replace your normal Movement. |

For full rules, templates, pricing, limits, and examples, see the dedicated **Power Catalogues**.

### Power Tags  

#### Tag: Charged  
Certain Powers require special preparation, materials, or focus that can only be sustained a few times each Safe Haven Rest.  
You have **Mastery Charges = your Mastery Rank** each Safe Haven Rest, this pool refreshes after a Safe Haven Rest.  
Only Powers with the **(Charged)** tag consume **1 Charge** on use, and you may activate **at most one Charged Power per round**.  


---
:
A Charged Power represents something you must **prepare, brew, or attune** beforehand — such as a potion, rune, transformation, or divine blessing.  
Once all Charges are spent, you cannot activate further (Charged) Powers until you rest and recover your daily focus.  

---
:
##### Converting Stones into Charges (Out of Combat)  
You may **burn 1 Stone** outside of combat to gain **+2 temporary Charges** till you take a Safe Haven Rest.  
The Stone is **lost until a Safe Haven Rest** and cannot be regenerated normally.  
These extra Charges remain until used or you regain your spent Stones.


#### Tag: Spell  
Any Power that interacts with the **Veil** the boundary between matter and will gains the **(Spell)** tag.  
Only abilities that explicitly reference “Spells” can target, dispel or suppress these effects.  

*Examples:* Ruin, Root, Bless, Hex Bolt, Healing Pulse.



}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Combat Core

Combat is divided into **Rounds** and **Turns**.

A **Round** is one full cycle in which every combatant gets one Turn.

A **Turn** is one combatant’s moment to act.

Initiative is rolled once at the start of combat.

Initiative Order normally remains fixed for the rest of the combat.
Initiative is not rolled again at the start of each Round unless a rule explicitly allows it.

---

### Dice Basics

All standard rolls use **d8s** unless a rule says otherwise.

---
When a die rolls an **8**, it explodes:

- roll that die again,
- add the new result to the 8,
- if the new roll is also an 8, repeat.

Damage dice do **not** explode unless a rule explicitly says otherwise.

---

### Dice Pools

Most rolls use the format: `XkY`

---
This means:

- roll **X dice**,
- keep the best **Y dice**,
- add the kept dice together.

For most tests:

- **Pool = relevant Attribute**
- **Keep = Mastery Rank**

---

Example:

---
A character with Might 8 and Mastery Rank 3 rolls: `8k3`

---
They roll 8 dice and keep the best 3.

---

### Minimum Pool

If your relevant Attribute is lower than your Mastery Rank, you may still roll a number of dice equal to your Mastery Rank.

Your Keep remains your Mastery Rank.

This means Mastery always represents practiced competence, even in a weak Attribute.

Example:

A character with Resolve 2 and Mastery Rank 4 rolls: `4k4`

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mastery and Keep

| **Mastery Rank** | **Keep** |
|:--:|:--:|
| I | k1 |
| II | k2 |
| III | k3 |
| IV | k4 |
| V | k5 |
| VI | k6 |
| VII | k7 |
| VIII | k8 |

---
:

## Raise

A character may declare Raises before making the roll for a Power. Each declared Raise creates a **Raise TN** that is **+4 higher** than the normal TN. The normal TN does not change.

| Roll Result | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Below normal TN | The Power fails. |
| Meets normal TN, but misses Raise TN | The Power succeeds, but the Raise Cost remains lost and no Raise Effects occur. |
| Meets Raise TN | The Power succeeds fully. The Raise Cost is restored and all declared Raise Effects occur. |

---
Raises are **all-or-nothing**. If you declare multiple Raises, you must meet the Raise TN for all declared Raises.

---

### Raise Cost

You must be able to pay the Raise Cost when declaring the Raise. The Raise Cost is taken from the Power before the roll. If the Raise succeeds, the cost is restored. If the Power succeeds but the Raise fails, the cost remains lost. A Power cannot be reduced below **0 Damage Dice** or below **Special(0)**. If a Special is reduced to 0 by Raise Cost, it is removed from the Power unless the Raise succeeds and restores it.

---

### Martial Raise Cost

For **Melee, Martial, and Ranged Martial Powers**:

---
**1 Raise costs MR Damage Dice.**

---
Example:  
A Mastery Rank 3 character pays **3d8 Damage** for 1 Raise.

---

### Spell Raise Cost

For **Spells**:

---
**1 Raise costs MR total value.**

---
This cost may be paid with Damage Dice, Special Value, or a mixture of both.

---
Example:  
A Mastery Rank 3 caster pays **3 total value**, such as:

- 3d8 Damage
- 3 Special Value
- 2d8 Damage and 1 Special Value
- 1d8 Damage and 2 Special Value

Spell Damage Dice from a Spell Focus count as Spell Damage Dice if they are part of the Spell's damage pool.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Martial Raise Options

| Cost | Raise Effect |
|---:|---|
| 1 Raise | Increase Damage by **+MR Damage Dice**. |
| 1 Raise | Increase one existing Special by **+MR**. |
| 1 Raise | Increase Range by **+4 m**. Only if the Power already has Range. |
| 2 Raises | Increase AoE Radius by **+1 m**. Only if the Power already has AoE. |

---
A Raise cannot add a new Special, Range, or AoE to a Power. No other Martial Raise Options belong to the default rules.

---

### Spell Raise Options

| Cost | Raise Effect |
|---:|---|
