| 1 Raise | Increase Spell Damage by **+1d8 Damage**. |
| 1 Raise | Increase one existing Special by **+MR**. |
| 1 Raise | Increase Range by **+4 m**. Only if the Spell already has Range. |
| 2 Raises | Increase AoE Radius by **+1 m**. Only if the Spell already has AoE. |
| 2 Raises | Increase Duration by **+1 Duration Step**. Only if the Spell already has Duration. |

---
A Raise cannot add a new Special, Range, AoE, or Duration to a Spell. No other Spell Raise Options belong to the default rules.
Spell Damage Raises are intentionally limited to **+1d8**. Spells scale primarily through Special Increase, Range, AoE, and Duration.

---

### Raise Example

A Mastery Rank 3 character uses a Power with:

- 8d8 Damage
- Ruin(3)

---
The character declares **1 Raise**.

---
Normal TN: 24  
Raise TN: 28  
Raise Cost: 3d8 Damage

---
Before the roll, the Power is treated as:

- 5d8 Damage
- Ruin(3)

| Roll Result | Outcome |
|---|---|
| 23 or lower | The Power fails. |
| 24 to 27 | The Power succeeds, but the Raise fails. It applies **5d8 Damage** and **Ruin(3)**. The Raise Cost remains lost. |
| 28+ | The Power succeeds fully. The Raise Cost is restored and the chosen Raise Effect applies. |

----
If the chosen Martial Raise Effect was **Damage**, the Power applies:

- 11d8 Damage
- Ruin(3)

---
If the chosen Martial Raise Effect was **Special Increase**, the Power applies:

- 8d8 Damage
- Ruin(6)

If this was a Spell and the chosen Spell Raise Effect was **Spell Damage**, the Spell would gain only **+1d8 Damage** instead.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Combat Senses

Combat Senses define how a character perceives the battlefield.

Every creature has ordinary senses: sight, hearing, touch, smell, taste, attention, instinct, and basic battlefield awareness. In the rules, this is called **Normal Combat Awareness**.

Normal Combat Awareness is the default. It is not a Passive. It does not require a Passive Slot. Every creature has it unless a condition, environment, Disadvantage, or effect removes or limits it.

Special Combat Senses are additional perception channels such as **Life Sense**, **Mage Sense**, **Tremor Sense**, **Sonar Sense** or **Predator Sense**. These senses allow a character to perceive things that Normal Combat Awareness may miss.

Combat Senses do not grant automatic success. If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, pressure, detail, or opposition matters, the GM may call for a normal **Perception** roll.

A Combat Sense defines what kind of information the character can access, not how well they roll.

---

## Sense Slot

Every character has one **Sense Slot**.

By default, it contains **Normal Combat Awareness**.

If a character gains a Special Combat Sense from any source, they may place it in this slot.

A Sense in the Sense Slot does not consume a Passive Slot.

Additional Special Combat Senses must be slotted as normal Passives, unless an Echo, Artifact, or specific rule says otherwise.

---

#### Example: Sense Slot

A character has no special senses.

Their Sense Slot contains:

**Normal Combat Awareness**

They perceive the battlefield through ordinary sight, hearing, attention, smell, touch, and instinct.

Later, the character gains **Life Sense** from an Echo.

They may place Life Sense in their Sense Slot.

Their Sense Slot now contains:

**Life Sense**

For sense-based rules, this character may use Life Sense as their active Combat Sense. They can still see, hear, and perceive normally unless something blocks their ordinary awareness.

If an enemy is invisible to Normal Combat Awareness but not invisible to Life Sense, this character may still attempt to perceive that enemy through Life Sense.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Perceiving and Targeting 

If a creature, object, trace, or phenomenon is valid for a Combat Sense, the character may perceive it through that Sense.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, combat pressure, or detail matters, roll **Perception**.

If the character clearly perceives a valid target through their active Combat Sense, they may target it through that Sense.

A Combat Sense does not ignore:

- range,
- cover,
- total cover,
- walls,
- line of effect,
- invalid targets,
- effects that block that Sense.

A Combat Sense only works against things it can actually perceive.

For example, Life Sense can perceive living creatures. It does not perceive constructs, lifeless objects, or illusions without life.

---

### Normal Combat Awareness

**Primary Channels:** Sight, Hearing, Touch, Smell/Taste  
**Range:** 60m

Normal Combat Awareness is the default perception of most creatures.

It represents ordinary sight, hearing, attention, body awareness, instinct, smell, taste, and ordinary battlefield reading.

Normal Combat Awareness can perceive visible enemies, sounds, movement, nearby vibrations, obvious smells, spoken words, weapons, cover, light, darkness, and ordinary battlefield threats.

If detail, stealth, concealment, distance, darkness, noise, smoke, fog, distraction, or pressure matters, roll Perception.

**Better Than Special Senses:**  
Normal Combat Awareness is broad. It can notice many ordinary things at once.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Normal Combat Awareness does not reliably perceive invisible creatures, hidden creatures, creatures behind total cover, creatures in heavy smoke or fog, or targets in complete darkness without help.

**Blocked By:**  
Darkness, Invisibility, heavy smoke, heavy fog, total cover, strong concealment, overwhelming noise, distraction, or effects that block ordinary perception.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Darkvision

**Primary Channel:** Sight  
**Range:** 30m

Darkvision is not a full Combat Sense. It is a minor sense upgrade.

Darkvision allows a creature to use Normal Combat Awareness through ordinary darkness within its listed range.

Darkvision does not reveal invisible creatures.

Darkvision does not see through fog, smoke, walls, total cover, magical darkness, illusions, or effects that directly block Normal Combat Awareness.

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Darkvision ignores ordinary darkness within range.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Invisible creatures, hidden creatures behind cover, creatures in heavy smoke or fog, magical concealment, illusions as false, or supernatural effects as supernatural.

**Blocked By:**  
Natural Darkness, Magical darkness, smoke, fog, walls, total cover, Invisibility, or effects that block sight.

---

### Life Sense

**Primary Channel:** Supernatural Body Sense  
**Range:** 30m

Life Sense perceives living creatures through heartbeat, breath, warmth, bloodflow, pulse, and vital force.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, or combat pressure matters, roll Perception.

If you clearly perceive a living creature through Life Sense, you may target it through Life Sense, unless another rule blocks targeting.

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Life Sense can work through darkness, poor visibility, smoke, fog, and ordinary Invisibility, as long as the target is alive and not protected against Life Sense.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Undead, constructs, lifeless objects, illusions without true life, corpses, purely magical effects, or creatures without living vital signs.

**Blocked By:**  
Invisibility against Life Sense, lack of life, total cover, strong barriers, artificial life-masking, undeath, construct nature, or effects that suppress vital signs.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mage Sense

**Primary Channel:** Supernatural  
**Range:** 30m

Mage Sense perceives active magic, spells, artifacts, curses, wards, magical barriers, summons, illusions, necromancy, undead force, divine marks, demonic marks, and supernatural effects.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, or detail matters, roll Perception.

If you clearly perceive a magical or supernatural target through Mage Sense, you may target it through Mage Sense, unless another rule blocks targeting.

Mage Sense includes what might otherwise be called Death Sense. Undead, necromancy, grave magic, divine marks, demonic signs, curses, and supernatural remnants are handled through Mage Sense.

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Mage Sense detects things that ordinary senses may not understand or perceive at all, such as invisible magical effects, illusions, curses, supernatural creatures, and magical objects.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Mundane stealth, normal creatures without a magical or supernatural signature, ordinary sounds, ordinary smells, normal darkness as visibility, nonmagical traps, or purely physical details.

**Blocked By:**  
Invisibility against Mage Sense, anti-magic effects, magical masking, suppressed aura, mundane nature, total cover, line of effect limits, or effects that hide supernatural signatures.

---

### Tremor Sense

**Primary Channel:** Touch  
**Range:** 20m

Tremor Sense perceives movement, weight, steps, impact, vibration, burrowing, pressure, and structural tension through a shared surface.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, or fine detail matters, roll Perception.

If you clearly perceive a creature through Tremor Sense, you may target it through Tremor Sense, unless another rule blocks targeting.

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Tremor Sense works without sight, without light, through smoke or fog, and against many invisible targets, as long as they interact with the same surface.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Flying creatures, hovering creatures, teleporting creatures, incorporeal creatures without vibration, creatures on disconnected surfaces, or targets that do not create readable vibration.

**Blocked By:**  
No shared surface, flight, hovering, levitation, teleportation, phasing, incorporeal movement, vibration isolation, soft or unstable ground, overwhelming ground vibration, magically silenced movement, or Invisibility against Tremor Sense.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Sonar Sense

**Primary Channel:** Hearing  
**Range:** 30m

Sonar Sense perceives echoes, reflected sound, room shape, solid bodies, openings, movement, obstacles, rough silhouettes, nearby body positions, and immediate spatial layout through sound.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, noise, or detail matters, roll Perception.

If you clearly perceive a creature through Sonar Sense, you may target it through Sonar Sense, unless another rule blocks targeting.

Sonar Sense is the standard Special Combat Sense for blind fighting, echolocation, close-range spatial reading, and sound-based battlefield perception.

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Sonar Sense works in darkness, smoke, fog, poor visibility, and without sight, as long as sound can travel and return useful information.

It can also help perceive nearby movement, openings, obstacles, and body positions in close combat.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Color, writing, facial expression, fine visual details, magic as magic, life as life, scent trails, ground vibration as structure, incorporeal creatures without echo, or targets that do not reflect readable sound.

**Blocked By:**  
Silence, overwhelming noise, echo confusion, sound absorption, soundproof barriers, dense walls, unusual acoustic environments, incorporeal targets, or Invisibility against Sonar Sense.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Predator Sense

**Primary Channel:** Smell/Taste and Instinctive Hunting Sense  
**Range:** 20m

Predator Sense perceives blood, wounds, fear, sweat, stress, sickness, weakness, prey-signs, recent passage, and hunting trails.

If uncertainty, stealth, concealment, distance, wind, contamination, or detail matters, roll Perception.

If you clearly perceive a creature through Predator Sense, you may target it through Predator Sense, unless another rule blocks targeting.

Predator Sense is not the same as Life Sense.

Life Sense says: "Something lives."

Predator Sense says: "Something bleeds, fears, weakens, flees, or can be hunted."

**Better Than Normal Combat Awareness:**  
Predator Sense is excellent for tracking, hunting, finding wounded targets, reading fear, following blood, or identifying vulnerable prey.

**Cannot Perceive:**  
Constructs, sterile illusions, scentless targets, sealed creatures, bloodless undead, purely magical effects, room shape, ground vibration, or creatures with no readable prey-sign.

**Blocked By:**  
Strong wind, rain, water, smoke, chemicals, sealed spaces, scent masking, lack of blood or fear, nonbiological nature, Invisibility against Predator Sense, or overwhelming environmental contamination.

---


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Combat Sense Summary

| Sense | Primary Channel | Range | Best Against | Fails Against |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| **Normal Combat Awareness** | Sight / Hearing / Touch / Smell | 60 m | Ordinary combat | Darkness, Invisibility, smoke, stealth, total cover |
| **Darkvision** | Sight | 30 m | Ordinary darkness | Invisibility, smoke, fog, walls, magical darkness |
| **Life Sense** | Supernatural Body Sense | 30 m | Living hidden targets | Undead, constructs, lifeless things |
| **Mage Sense** | Supernatural | 30 m | Magic, undead, illusions, curses | Mundane stealth, nonmagical creatures |
| **Tremor Sense** | Touch | 20 m | Grounded invisible targets | Flying, hovering, incorporeal, no shared surface |
| **Sonar Sense** | Hearing | 30 m | Darkness, fog, smoke, blind fighting | Silence, noise, sound absorption, incorporeal targets |
| **Predator Sense** | Smell/Taste + Instinct | 20 m | Blood, wounds, fear, tracking | Constructs, sterile illusions, sealed spaces |

---

### Invisibility and Sense Blocking

Invisibility and similar effects do not make a creature impossible to perceive by every possible method.

Instead, they block specific senses.

Basic Invisibility blocks **Normal Combat Awareness**.

This means the creature cannot be perceived or targeted through ordinary sight, ordinary combat attention, or Darkvision.

At higher levels, an Invisibility effect may block additional Combat Senses.

Example:

**Silent Veil**  
Blocks Normal Combat Awareness.  
At higher levels, it also blocks Sonar Sense and Tremor Sense.

This represents a creature that is unseen, silent, and vibrationless.

Example:

**Hollow Veil**  
Blocks Normal Combat Awareness.  
At higher levels, it also blocks Life Sense, Predator Sense, and Mage Sense.

This represents a creature that is unseen, unreadable as living prey, and hidden from magical perception.

A creature protected against a Sense cannot be perceived or targeted through that Sense unless a specific rule says otherwise.

Darkness, Silence, Null Fields, magical concealment, anti-sense effects, and other battlefield powers may use the same structure.

They define which senses they block.
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Stealth, Hidden, and Invisibility
Stealth is the skill of hiding your presence, moving quietly, controlling traces, using cover, timing movement, and avoiding attention.
Hidden is the state a creature gains when it is successfully concealed from observers.
Invisibility is a Combat Sense effect. It blocks one or more Combat Senses and increases the Perception TN required to locate, target, or read the invisible creature.
A creature can be Hidden without being invisible.
A creature can be invisible without being Hidden through Stealth.
A creature can also be both invisible and benefiting from Stealth.


### Stealth Without Invisibility
Any creature may attempt to become Hidden when it tries to hide, sneak past observers, remain unnoticed, or conceal its position.
Make one Stealth Check for the scene or situation.
Use the normal Skill Check TN for the creature's Mastery Rank unless the GM sets a different situational TN.
On a success, the creature becomes Hidden.
For each Raise on the Stealth Check, increase the Perception TN to detect or locate the Hidden creature by +2.
This bonus is called the Stealth Raise Bonus.
On a failure, the creature is not Hidden. Observers can perceive it normally through their available Combat Senses.
A creature that is not Hidden does not force a Perception Check just because it attempted Stealth and failed.

### Invisibility Without Stealth
An invisible creature is difficult to locate, target, and read through the Combat Senses its Invisibility blocks.
When a creature tries to locate, target, or read an invisible creature through a blocked Combat Sense, it must make a Perception Check.
The TN is:
**Perception TN = normal Skill Check TN by the invisible creature's Mastery Rank + current Invisibility Bonus**

### Invisibility With Stealth
An invisible creature may also attempt Stealth.
Make one Stealth Check for the scene or situation.
The Stealth Check does not decide whether the creature is invisible.
The Stealth Check only determines whether the creature gains a Stealth Raise Bonus.
For each Raise on the Stealth Check, increase the Perception TN to detect, locate, target, or read the invisible creature by +2.
If the Stealth Check fails, the creature gains no Stealth Raise Bonus, but it is still invisible.
Use:
**Perception TN = normal Skill Check TN by the invisible creature's Mastery Rank + current Invisibility Bonus + Stealth Raise Bonus**
### When Perception Happens
A creature does not automatically get a Perception Check just because another creature is nearby.
A Perception Check happens when there is a reason to notice, locate, target, or read the creature.
Common reasons include: the creature moves through a watched area, tries to sneak past an observer, makes noise or leaves clear signs, attacks, is actively searched for, or is directly targeted.
If there is no reason to suspect anything and no clear sensory clue, no Perception Check happens.
### Targeting an Invisible Creature
Before a creature can directly target an invisible creature with an attack, Spell, or hostile Power, check whether it can perceive the invisible creature through an unblocked Combat Sense.
If it can, it may target the creature normally.
If its relevant Combat Sense is blocked, it must make a Perception Check.
On a success, it locates the invisible creature until the end of its next turn and may target it normally during that time.
On a failure, it cannot directly target that creature this turn.
The action is not spent.
The creature must choose another legal target or another legal action.

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Attacking from Invisibility
When an invisible creature attacks a target, check whether the target can perceive or read the attacker through an unblocked Combat Sense.
If it can, the target uses full Evade.
If its relevant Combat Sense is blocked, the target makes a Perception Check.
On a success, the target uses full Evade against attacks from that invisible creature until the end of that invisible creature's turn.
On a failure, the target uses half Evade against attacks from that invisible creature until the end of that invisible creature's turn.
After the attack is declared, apply Cloak Disruption.
Any Stealth Raise Bonus is lost.
### Losing the Stealth Raise Bonus
The Stealth Raise Bonus is lost when the creature reveals its position or stops moving carefully.
The Stealth Raise Bonus is always lost when the creature makes a direct attack, casts a hostile Spell, uses a hostile Power, uses a visible Reaction, takes final HP damage, or voluntarily moves more than 3 m.
Once the Stealth Raise Bonus is lost, observers know enough about the creature's position that the same Stealth attempt no longer applies.
The creature cannot simply roll Stealth again in the same situation.
To gain a new Stealth Raise Bonus, the situation must meaningfully change: new cover, darkness, smoke, distraction, broken line of awareness, changed terrain, or another real opportunity to hide.
### Cloak Disruption
Movement, violence, damage, and visible reactions disrupt Invisibility.
Whenever your Cloak is disrupted, reduce your current Invisibility Bonus until the start of your next turn.
Your Invisibility Bonus cannot be reduced below 0.
At the start of your next turn, your Invisibility Bonus returns to its normal value.
| **Action / Event** | **Invisibility Bonus Reduction** |
|:--|:--:|
| Move up to 3 m | no reduction |
| For every full 4 m voluntarily moved | −4 |
| Make a direct attack | −4 |
| Cast a hostile Spell | −4 |
| Use a hostile Power | −4 |
| Use a Reaction that directly affects another creature | −4 |
| Take final HP damage | −4 |

---
These reductions stack.
If the creature has a Stealth Raise Bonus, moving more than 3 m removes that Stealth Raise Bonus.
Cloak Disruption reduces only the current Invisibility Bonus.
### Combat Perception Limit
A creature may make only one Perception Check per Hidden or invisible creature per round unless a rule says otherwise.
If the check succeeds, the result applies until the end of the observer's next turn.
If the check is made against an invisible attacker, the result applies against that invisible creature's attacks until the end of that invisible creature's turn.

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Examples
**Stealth without Invisibility.** A creature hides in a room and succeeds on its Stealth Check with 2 Raises. Its Stealth Raise Bonus is +4. A guard searching the room rolls Perception against the creature's normal Skill Check TN +4. If the Stealth Check had failed, the creature would not be Hidden and the guard would perceive it normally.

---
**Invisibility without Stealth.** An invisible creature stands still in a room. A guard has a reason to search the room. The guard rolls Perception against the creature's normal Skill Check TN + current Invisibility Bonus.

---
**Invisibility with Stealth.** An invisible creature stands still and also succeeds on Stealth with 2 Raises. A guard searching the room rolls Perception against the creature's normal Skill Check TN + current Invisibility Bonus +4.

---
**Invisible creature moves 8 m.** The creature moves 8 m while invisible. Its current Invisibility Bonus is reduced by −8 until the start of its next turn. If it had a Stealth Raise Bonus, that bonus is lost because it moved more than 3 m.

---
**Invisible creature attacks.** The target makes a Perception Check. On a success, it uses full Evade. On a failure, it uses half Evade. The invisible creature suffers −4 Invisibility Bonus from Cloak Disruption, and any Stealth Raise Bonus is lost.

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Round Structure

Each combat follows this structure:

1. **Start of Combat**
   - Roll Initiative.
   - Resolve the initial Initiative Shop.
   - Determine Initiative Order.

2. **Resolve Turns**
   - Creatures act in Initiative Order.
   - Each creature takes one Turn per Round.

3. **End the Round**
   - Resolve end-of-round effects.
   - Regenerate Exhausted Stones as normal.

4. **Start a New Round**
   - Initiative Order remains unchanged.
   - Initiative is not rolled again unless a rule explicitly allows it.
   - Some effects, such as Wits Stone Powers, may reopen the Initiative Shop.
---

### Basic Action Kit

At the start of each Round, each character normally has:

- **1 Movement**
- **1 Attack Action**
- **1 Reaction**

Some Powers, Items, Stones, or Initiative Shop options may grant additional Movements, Attack Actions, or Reactions.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Turn Structure

On your Turn, resolve the following steps in order:

1. **Start of Turn**
2. **Movement and Attack Action**
3. **End of Turn**

---

### Start of Turn

At the start of your Turn, resolve all effects that trigger at the start of your Turn.

This includes:

- ongoing damage,
- Diminishing Stack ticks,
- regeneration,
- start-of-turn buffs,
- start-of-turn penalties,
- and similar effects.

If an effect has Diminishing Stacks, resolve it in this order:

1. **Tick** the effect.
2. **Decay** the stack value by 1.

Example:

`Lacerate(4)` deals its start-of-turn effect, then becomes `Lacerate(3)`.

Diminishing Stacks cannot decay below 0.

---

### Movement and Attack Action

During your Turn, you may use:

- **1 Movement**
- **1 Attack Action**

You may use them in either order unless a rule says otherwise.

A Movement may be normal Movement or a Movement Power.

An Attack Action may be used to:

- make a weapon attack,
- use an Active Power,
- cast a Spell,
- activate an Active Buff,
- use an item,
- or perform another action that costs an Attack Action.

---

### Reactions

A Reaction is used outside your Turn when its trigger occurs.

You normally have **1 Reaction per Round**.

A Reaction is not a second Turn.

A Reaction is not a free Attack Action unless a rule explicitly says so.

If you gain an extra Reaction, you may use it only if a valid trigger occurs.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### End of Turn

At the end of your Turn, resolve effects that trigger at the end of your Turn.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Initiative

Initiative represents combat tempo, reaction speed, and the ability to seize openings.

Initiative is rolled once at the **start of combat**.

Roll a number of d8s equal to your **Mastery Rank**.

Keep all dice.

8s explode.

Your Initiative Score is:

`Initiative Roll + Combat Reflexes spent`

You may spend Combat Reflexes Skill Points like a normal Skill resource after seeing the roll, up to your remaining Combat Reflexes pool and no more than `MR × 4` per Initiative roll.

---

### Initiative Order

After all characters have rolled Initiative and finished spending from the Initiative Shop, remaining Initiative Scores determine turn order.

Higher Initiative acts first.

Your Initiative Score normally remains fixed for the rest of the combat.

Initiative is not rolled again at the start of each Round unless a rule explicitly allows it.

---

### Initiative Shop

After rolling Initiative, you may spend points from your Initiative Score.

| **Cost** | **Effect** |
|---:|---|
| 4 | Gain +2 m Movement this Round. |
| 8 | Initiative Swap: choose another willing player. You and that player swap Initiative Scores. |
| 12 | Gain +1 extra Reaction this Round. Maximum once per Round. |
| 16 | Remove 1d8 Stress from your current Stress Bar. |
| 20 | Gain +1 extra Attack Action this Round. Maximum once per Round. |

After spending, your remaining Initiative Score determines your place in Initiative Order.

Initiative can never drop below 0.

The Initiative Shop is normally available only immediately after the initial Initiative roll at the start of combat.

---

### Additional Initiative Shops

The Initiative Shop does not open automatically at the start of every Round.

Some effects, especially **Wits Stone Powers**, may allow a character to roll Initiative again and reopen the Initiative Shop during combat.

If a rule allows this, follow that rule’s timing and cost.

See **Wits Stone Powers** for additional Initiative Shop access.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Attacks

An attack is any action that tries to hit a target through an attack roll. 
This includes weapon attacks and attack-based Active Powers.
Spells follow the Magic rules, but still use the same core dice and Raise engine.

---
:
### Defense Structure

The Mastery System separates defense into four **Primary Defense Pillars** and four specialized **Premium Defense Systems**.

---
The Primary Defense Pillars are complete defensive foundations around which a character may build their combat identity.

| **Primary Defense Pillar** | **Stage** | **Function** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Parry** | Before the Attack Roll | Removes Attack Dice from an eligible direct Attack. If the entire Attack Pool is reduced to 0, the Attack is Fully Parried and does not resolve. |
| **Evade** | During the Attack Roll | Determines the Target Number a direct Attack must reach to hit the character. Evade does not modify the TN of an AoE Attack.|
| **Armor** | After the Damage Roll | Reduces the rolled damage result before it is applied to the character. |
| **Absorption** | After actual HP loss | Provides additional real HP and converts actual HP lost into Temporary Colorless Stones. |

Premium Defense Systems are powerful specialized additions. They normally do not replace a complete Primary Defense Pillar. Instead, they support and modify an existing defensive build.

| **Premium Defense System** | **Stage** | **Function** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Phasing** | After the Attack hits | Ignores the complete triggering hit, including its damage and applicable hit effects. |
| **Ward** | Before an eligible Special is applied | Reduces every incoming eligible hostile Special(X). If the Special is reduced to 0 or less, it is not applied. |
| **Damage Negation** | Before the Damage Roll | Removes Damage Dice from an incoming Damage Pool. Damage Negation cannot remove more than half of the original Damage Dice. |
| **Damage Reduction** | After Armor | Reduces the remaining damage after Armor has been applied. |

**Spell Resistance** is a separate specialized defense against Powers with the **Spell** tag. Spell Resistance increases the Final Spell TN against the protected creature. It does not reduce Attack Dice, Damage Dice, damage, or Specials.

---
:
### Choosing Your Defense

Do not worry about using every defensive system listed above. Most characters focus on **one Primary Defense Pillar** and may later support it with a second Pillar or one of the specialized Premium Defenses.

---
:
A heavily armored character may rely primarily on **Armor**, while a fast character builds around **Evade**. A dedicated defender may choose **Parry**, while an Absorption character deliberately accepts actual HP loss and turns it into power. Premium Defenses such as Damage Negation, Damage Reduction, Phasing, or Ward are usually added to strengthen one of these foundations rather than replace it.

---
:
You are not expected to track every step in the sequence below. When resolving an effect, simply skip every step for which your character has no relevant defense. A character without Parry ignores the Parry step; a character without Ward ignores the Ward step; and a character without Absorption ends the sequence after damage has been applied.

---
:
Especially when building a new character, it is usually better to develop **one clear defensive identity first** instead of investing in several incomplete defenses.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

<div style="text-align: justify;">

### Complete Attack and Defense Sequence

When resolving an Attack, Spell, or another hostile effect, follow the applicable steps below. Effects that do not make an Attack Roll, deal damage, or apply Specials may skip the corresponding steps.

#### 1. Choose the Target and Effect

Choose the target and the Attack, Spell, Power, weapon, or other hostile effect being used. Confirm that the target is valid, within Range, and can legally be affected by the chosen effect.

#### 2. Determine the Target Number

Determine whether the effect is a direct Attack or an AoE Attack.

---
 - A direct normal Attack rolls against the target’s Evade.
 - A direct Spell uses Final Spell TN = Spell Base TN + Target Spell Resistance.
 - An AoE Attack uses Area TN = 8 × Source Mastery Rank.
---
AoE Attacks ignore the Evade and Spell Resistance of creature

#### 3. Declare Raises and Pay Costs

Declare all Raises before rolling, pay any required costs, and choose all Raise Effects. Each declared Raise increases the required Target Number by **+4**.

#### 4. Build the Attack or Spell Pool

Build the appropriate dice pool from the relevant Attribute, weapon, Power, and all other legal modifiers. The acting character normally keeps dice equal to their Mastery Rank.

#### 5. Apply Parry

Before an eligible direct Attack is rolled, a character currently using Parry may spend points from their Parry Pool. Each point removes **1 Attack Die** from the Attack Pool assigned to that character. If fewer dice remain than the normal Keep value, roll and keep all remaining dice. If the Attack Pool is reduced to **0 Attack Dice**, the Attack is Fully Parried: no Attack Roll is made, the Attack does not hit, no damage is dealt, no Specials are applied, and no effect requiring the Attack to hit is resolved.

#### 6. Make the Roll

Roll the remaining Attack or Spell Pool, resolve exploding dice, keep the appropriate number of dice, and compare the final result to the applicable Target Number. If the result does not reach the Target Number, the effect misses or fails against that target and its resolution ends there.For an AoE Attack, compare the result once against the Area TN. On a success, every valid non-excluded target inside the area is hit. Do not compare the result separately against individual targets.

#### 7. Resolve Phasing

After an Attack successfully hits, but before its damage, Specials, or other hit effects are applied, the target may use an eligible Phasing effect. If the hit is ignored through Phasing, no damage is rolled, no Specials are applied, and no other effect requiring the hit is resolved against that target.

#### 8. Resolve Hit Effects

Resolve forced movement, control effects, conditions, and other hit effects according to their individual rules.

A successful Attack, Spell, or AoE does not automatically trigger a second defensive roll. A later Attribute Check occurs only if the effect explicitly grants a break attempt, disbelief attempt, or another separately timed response.


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### 9. Apply Ward to Incoming Specials

Before an eligible hostile **Special(X)** is applied, reduce its value by the target's Ward. Ward applies separately to every eligible Special delivered by the same Attack or effect. If a Special is reduced to **0 or less**, it is not applied. Ward does not reduce Specials already affecting the target; existing Specials may instead be reduced or removed through Cleanse.
#### 10. Build the Damage Pool

Determine the complete Damage Pool assigned to the target, including weapon damage, Power damage, successful Raise damage, bonus Damage Dice, and all other legal damage modifiers.

#### 11. Apply Damage Negation

Before the Damage Pool is rolled, the target may spend Damage Negation. Each point spent removes **1 Damage Die** from the Damage Pool assigned to that target. All Damage Negation effects combined can never remove more than half of the original Damage Dice, rounded down. Damage Negation does not cause the Attack to miss and does not prevent Specials or other hit effects.

#### 12. Roll Damage

Roll all remaining Damage Dice. Damage Dice do not explode unless a rule explicitly states otherwise.

#### 13. Apply Penetration and Armor

Penetration first reduces the amount of Armor that applies against the Attack. Subtract the remaining Armor from the rolled damage result using `Damage after Armor = Rolled Damage − Remaining Armor`. Armor does not normally reduce Specials, conditions, or ongoing Special damage unless a rule explicitly states otherwise.

#### 14. Apply Damage Reduction

Apply Damage Reduction to the damage remaining after Armor. If several Damage Reduction effects apply, resolve them according to the specific Damage Reduction rules.

#### 15. Apply Minimum Damage from Natural 8s

If Armor and Damage Reduction reduce the damage to **0 or less**, the target still takes **1 damage for each natural 8 rolled on the original Damage Dice**. Only dice that were actually rolled count; Damage Dice removed through Damage Negation cannot generate minimum damage.

#### 16. Apply Temporary HP and Other Damage Buffers

Apply the remaining damage to Temporary HP or another legal damage buffer before applying it to the target's real HP. Damage assigned to Temporary HP is not actual HP loss.

#### 17. Apply Damage to Health

Apply all remaining damage to the target's current Health Bar according to the normal Health and Health Level rules.

#### 18. Resolve Absorption

After the damage instance has been completely resolved, determine how many actual HP were removed from the target's Health Bars. Only actual HP loss may trigger Absorption. Damage prevented by Parry, Evade, Phasing, Damage Negation, Armor, Damage Reduction, Temporary HP, resistance, immunity, or another defensive effect does not count. Any Temporary Colorless Stones generated through Absorption are gained after the triggering damage instance has finished resolving.

</div>

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Armor and Shields

Armor reduces incoming damage.

Your total Armor is:

`Armor Value + Shield Value + Mastery Rank + other Armor bonuses`

Subtract total Armor from incoming damage.

Armor applies to attacks and damage instances unless a rule says the damage ignores Armor.

Ongoing Special damage usually ignores Armor.

---

### Armor Types

| **Armor Type** | **Armor Value** | **Evade** | **Initiative** | **Skill Penalty** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **Light** | +4 | +0 | — | — |
| **Medium** | +8 | −2 | −4 | −1d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Heavy** | +12 | −4 | −8 | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |

---

### Shield Types

| **Shield Type** | **Armor Value** | **Evade** | **Initiative** | **Skill Penalty** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **Parry Shield** | +1 | +4 | — | −1d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Medium Shield** | +4 | +0 | — | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Tower Shield** | +8 | −4 | −4 | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |

---


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Attribute Checks Against Effects

Resistance is resolved through explicitly named Attribute Checks. There is no separate universal defensive roll category.

When a rule gives a creature a chance to resist, endure, disbelieve, or break an effect, that rule names the exact Attribute to roll.

---

### Resistance Attributes

| **What is being resisted** | **Attribute** |
|:--|:--|
| Bodily endurance, poison, disease, pain, physical collapse, or effects that test whether the body holds together | **Vitality** |
| Corruption, possession, divine or demonic pressure, soul binding, or effects directed at the soul | **Resolve** |
| Structured thought, memory, logic, mental analysis, or recognizing a constructed contradiction | **Intellect** |
| Immediate awareness, instinct, sensory doubt, mental intrusion, or noticing that perception has been manipulated | **Wits** |

**Might, Agility, and Influence are not resistance Attributes.** They are not used to resist hostile effects unless a rule is clearly resolving a different kind of action rather than resistance.

A Power must name **Vitality**, **Resolve**, **Intellect**, or **Wits** directly. It never lists Body, Mind, or Spirit as a roll category.

---

### Attribute Check Resolution

When an effect calls for an Attribute Check:

1. Roll dice equal to the named Attribute.
2. Keep dice equal to your Mastery Rank.
3. Apply normal Pool modifiers unless the effect says otherwise.
4. Compare the result to the TN stated by the effect.

Skill Points do not apply unless the rule explicitly names a Skill Check.

There is no Vitality Reserve expenditure, and no universal reroll or automatic-success mechanic for these checks.

If a hostile effect calls for an Attribute Check but does not state a TN, use:

`Attribute Check TN = 8 × Source Mastery Rank`

---

### No Automatic Second Roll

A successful Attack, Spell, or AoE normally resolves its full listed payload.

Push, Pull, Knockback, Prone, Root, Disarm, and Stunned do not automatically grant an Attribute Check. Ongoing effects do not automatically grant repeated checks.

An Attribute Check is used only when a rule explicitly creates a separately timed response, such as:

- spending an Action to inspect an illusion,
- attempting to break a mental or spiritual binding,
- resisting unwilling telepathic contact,
- making a Death Check,
- or resolving a Stress Breakdown Check.


---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Advantage and Disadvantage

Some effects grant Advantage or impose Disadvantage.

Advantage and Disadvantage modify how dice behave.

They do not change Pool or Keep values.

---

### Advantage

When rolling with Advantage, any **1** rolled in the basic dice pool may be rerolled once.

This applies only to the initial dice pool.

It does not apply to explosion dice.

The new result replaces the 1, even if the new result is another 1.

Explosions are then resolved normally.

---

### Disadvantage

When rolling with Disadvantage, only your single highest exploding die may explode.

All other dice that roll 8 count as flat 8s and do not explode.

If multiple dice roll 8, choose one die to explode.

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


### Health & Stress Bars
---

Damage does not only bring a character closer to defeat.  
It also reduces how much of their full potential they can still bring into a roll.

Your **Vitality** determines the size of your Health Bars.  
Each Health Bar represents a deeper stage of injury.

---

#### Determining Health Bars

Everyone has five Health Bars.

Each Health Bar has:

<strong>Vitality × 2</strong> boxes.

After all five Health Bars are depleted, the character enters the **Incapacitated** state, which is represented by one final box.

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Active Health Bar:</strong> the highest Health Bar that is not Scarred.</li>
  <li>You can heal damage only within your current Active Health Bar.</li>
  <li>Once a Health Bar is completely filled, it becomes <strong>Scarred</strong>.</li>
  <li>A Scarred Health Bar cannot be healed during the same combat unless a rule explicitly allows it.</li>
  <li>The final box is always <strong>Incapacitated</strong>.</li>
</ul>

---

#### Health Penalties

Health penalties reduce the number of dice you roll.

| Health | Healthy | Bruised | Injured | Wounded | Broken | Incap. |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Pool** | — | −10% | −20% | −40% | −50% | no actions |

Round dice loss down, minimum 0.

Applies to attacks, Attribute Checks, Skill Checks, spells, rituals, and initiative.  
Does not reduce damage dice unless a rule explicitly says so.

---

#### Order of Pool Reduction

When multiple rules modify a dice pool, apply them in this order:

1. Start with the relevant Attribute.
2. Apply Skill training rules.
   - If the Skill is trained enough, use the full Attribute pool.
   - If the Skill is too low, use half the Attribute pool, rounded down.
3. Apply all flat pool changes from range, environment, Powers, Stones, Buffs, Specials, or other effects. This includes **Weaken** and **Soulburn**.
4. Apply the current percentage-based **Health Penalty** to the remaining pool.
5. Round the number of dice lost to the Health Penalty down.
6. Apply the final **Minimum Pool Rule**: the pool cannot be reduced below your **Mastery Rank**.
7. Roll the final pool and keep dice equal to Mastery Rank, unless a rule explicitly modifies Keep.

Flat pool reductions are applied before the percentage-based Health Penalty. If multiple flat reductions apply to the same roll, add them together before calculating the Health Penalty.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Example

Someone with **Vitality 4** has the following Health Levels.

Each normal Health Bar has:

<strong>Vitality × 2 = 8 boxes</strong>

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  -  Healthy [Vitality × 2] (No penalty)

--- 
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Bruised [Vitality × 2] (−10% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Injured [Vitality × 2] (−20% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Wounded [Vitality × 2] (−40% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Broken [Vitality × 2] (−50% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] - Incapacitated

---

#### Pool Reduction Example

A character has **Agility 16**, **Mastery Rank 3**, and is making a **Stealth** check. Their Stealth Skill is high enough to use the full Attribute Pool, but they are affected by **Weaken(4)** and are currently **Injured**.

1. Attribute Pool = 16  
2. Skill trained enough → full pool = 16  
3. Weaken(4) removes 4 dice → 12 dice remain  
4. Injured Health Penalty removes 20% of 12 → lose 2 dice  
5. Final Pool = **10k3**

If the same character were **Wounded**, the 40% Health Penalty would remove 4 dice from the post-Weaken pool of 12.

Final Pool = **8k3**

---

### Healing & Recovery

Healing — whether through magic, rest, Stones, or other effects — can only restore damage within the current <strong>Active Health Bar</strong>.

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>Once a <strong>Health Bar</strong> is completely filled, it becomes <strong>Scarred</strong>.</li>
  <li>A Scarred Health Bar is skipped. The next lower Health Bar becomes the new Active Health Bar.</li>
  <li>Healing can reduce damage <strong>within</strong> the current Active Health Bar.</li>
  <li>Healing cannot reset, reopen, or erase a Scarred Health Bar unless a rule explicitly says so.</li>
  <li>Only <strong>long-term rest</strong>, <strong>ritual healing</strong>, or <strong>Stone expenditure</strong> may restore Scarred Health Bars. See Rest & Recovery for details.</li>
</ul>

---

### Incapacitation & Death

When all of your Health Bars are fully depleted, your body collapses under the weight of battle.

You are now **Incapacitated**.

#### Incapacitated

- You fall **Prone**.
- You cannot take **Actions, Reactions, or Movement**.
- You begin with **0 Death Marks**.
- At the end of each of your turns, the GM rolls your **Death Check**.
  *(The roll may be kept secret for dramatic effect.)*

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


### Death Check
Use the higher of your **Vitality** or **Resolve**.

Roll dice equal to that Attribute and keep dice equal to your **Mastery Rank**.

`Death Check TN = 8 × your Mastery Rank`

A Death Check is a special Attribute Check.

- Skill Points cannot be spent on it.
- Vitality cannot be spent after the roll.
- Health penalties do not reduce the Death Check Pool.
- Only an effect that explicitly modifies Death Checks can change the result.

| Result | Effect |
|:--|:--|
| **Success** | You remain Incapacitated and mark **1 Success**. |
| **Failure** | You gain **1 Death Mark** (Failure). |

Track both <strong>Successes</strong> and <strong>Death Marks</strong> separately:
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>At <strong>4 Successes</strong>, you are <strong>Stabilized</strong>.</li>
  <li>At <strong>4 Death Marks</strong>, you <strong>die</strong>.</li>
</ul>

### Stabilized
Once <strong>4 Successes</strong> are reached or an ally stabilizes you, you are <strong>Stable</strong>.
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>You remain <strong>Unconscious</strong> at <strong>0 Health Bars</strong>.</li>
  <li>You no longer roll Death Checks.</li>
  <li>You remain at <strong>0 boxes</strong> and do not awaken until healing or recovery restores you.</li>
  <li>If you receive <strong>any healing</strong>, you wake <strong>immediately</strong> with that amount restored.</li>
</ul>

### Healing & Assistance
An adjacent ally may:
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>Spend <strong>1 Full Action</strong> to make a Medicine Skill Check against a Difficult TN based on the patient’s Mastery Rank (<code>TN = 12 × MR</code>). On success, the patient is Stabilized (counts as <strong>4 Successes</strong>).</li>
  <li>Use <strong>any Healing Spell, Power, or Aura</strong> to restore HP / end incapacitation once <code>HP &gt; 0</code>.</li>
  <li>Passive or continuous <strong>Healing Auras</strong> restore you the moment your Health Bars rise above <code>0</code>.</li>
</ul>

> *When light touches flesh, even the dying remember what it means to breathe.*


---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Stress Points
---

Stress mirrors Health, but breaking the mind doesn’t kill you, it imposes mental collapse.


#### Example

Someone with **Resolve 3** and **Intellect 5** has the following Stress Levels.

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] -  Healthy [Resolve + Intellect] (No penalty)

--- 
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Stressed [Resolve + Intellect] (No penalty)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Not Well [Resolve + Intellect] (No penalty)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] - Breaking [Resolve + Intellect]  (No penalty)

---
:
[ ] - Breakdown


::
*Recovery* is harder than HP: safe rest, rituals, therapy/roleplay scenes, rare items.


#### Stress Healing:
Some Stone Powers or Mastery Tree allow you to heal Stress instead of HP.

- Stress may only be healed down to the current start of your Stress Bar.

- A Stress Bar that is already filled cannot be reduced or undone.

- Stress Healing cannot “reset” a full bar, but it can reduce Stress within the current bar.


### Stress Breakdown Check

Whenever your Stress Track fills all bars, make a **Wits Attribute Check** and keep dice equal to your Mastery Rank.

`Stress Breakdown TN = 8 × your Mastery Rank`

Skill Points and Vitality expenditure do not apply.

#### Results
- **Success (Virtue)** → You reset your Stress Track back to *Clear* and gain a short burst of clarity or courage. For the next action you gain another die to keep to your roll. 


- **Failure (Affliction)** → Your Stress Track clears, but you gain an  **Affliction**.  
  - Multiple Afflictions may stack.  

When the **Breakdown** box is reached, the character suffers a **Meltdown** (catatonia, panic, or possession as decided by the GM).

#### Failure
Your Stress Track resets back to **Clear**, and you choose ONE:

**A) Scar of Will (Player Choice) — Mental Restriction**
- You choose a **Mental Restriction (2 Points)** (Oath, Fear, or Trait).
- This is **added** to your character (it’s not rolled and not forced).
- Rule stays the same: acting against it requires `Resolve k1`
  (TN 6 minor / TN 10 severe / TN 14 core violation).
- Reward: immediately recover 2 Reroll Points, up to your maximum..

}}




\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

**B) Push It Down (GM Pressure) — Misfortune Tokens**
- You take no new restriction.
- Instead the GM gains **1d8 Misfortune Tokens**.
- The GM may spend a token to:
  - **force a reroll** of one player roll (reroll all dice; new result stands), OR
  - **introduce a credible complication/obstacle** tied to the scene,
  - **worsen a failed roll** (make the consequence sharper).
- Limits: max **1 token spent per roll**.


---



}}




\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Combat Maneuvers

---
Combat maneuvers add tactical depth to each round.<br>
Every character can perform <strong>1 Movement</strong>, <strong>1 Attack Action</strong>, and <strong>1 Reaction</strong> per round (unless otherwise stated).<br>
Maneuvers are divided into <strong>Movements</strong>, <strong>Reactions</strong> (instant responses), and <strong>Actions</strong> (deliberate moves or stances).

---
:
#### Basic Attack (No Power)
When you make an attack <strong>without using a Power</strong>, it is a <strong>Basic Attack</strong>:
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Damage:</strong> Weapon Damage Dice + Passives + Active Buffs.</li>
  <li><strong>No Power Effects:</strong> You may not add Power effects, riders, or Power-only modifiers.</li>
  <li><strong>Extra Attacks:</strong> Extra Attacks gained from Stones/Initiative are allowed, but they are also <strong>Basic Attacks</strong> unless you activate a Power.</li>
</ul>

---
:

#### Threatened Ranged

If **at least one enemy is within their melee reach**, and you declare a **Ranged Power** (**bow**, **crossbow** or **thrown weapon**), the attack is **Threatened**:

- You make the Ranged Attack with **Disadvantage**.
- After you declare the attack, **all** enemies within your melee reach may immediately spend their **Reactions**.


---
:
### Movement
Your <strong>Movement</strong> represents your tactical positioning during your turn.
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Move Distance:</strong> You can move up to your <strong>Speed</strong> (in meters).</li>
  <li><strong>Draw / Sheathe:</strong> You may draw or sheathe a weapon as part of your Movement.</li>
  <li><strong>No Splitting:</strong> If you use a <strong>Movement Maneuver</strong> or a <strong>Movement Power</strong> (such as <em>Dash</em>), it uses up your entire Movement and <strong>cannot be split</strong>.</li>
</ul>

---
:
#### Movement Options
<em>(Choose one use of <strong>Movement</strong> per turn, unless a Power says otherwise.)</em>

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Move</strong> – Move up to your <strong>Speed</strong> this turn.
      This movement <strong>provokes Reactions</strong> as normal.</li>

  <li><strong>Dash</strong> – Focus entirely on movement.
      Move up to <strong>double your normal Speed</strong> this turn.
      You <strong>cannot perform</strong> your <strong>base Attack Action</strong>, but you may still buy additional attacks using Stones.<br>
      This movement <strong>provokes Reactions</strong> as normal.</li>

  <li><strong>Disengage</strong> – Withdraw carefully.
      Move up to your <strong>normal Speed</strong> this turn <strong>without provoking Reactions</strong>.<br>
      You cannot perform your <strong>base Attack Action</strong> this turn (unless granted by a Stone).</li>

  <li><strong>Quick Load</strong> – Convert movement into reloading.
      Instead of moving, you may spend your <strong>Movement</strong> to perform <strong>Reload (1)</strong>.
      You may convert Movement into Reload multiple times in the same turn (if you gain additional Movement), up to a maximum of <strong>your Mastery Rank (MR)</strong> total Reload per turn.
      You still need <strong>one free hand</strong> and must be able to manipulate the weapon/ammunition.<br>
      You cannot Quick Load while <strong>Immobilized/Restrained</strong>.</li>

  <li><strong>Stand Up</strong> – Recover from a Prone state.
      Standing up costs <strong>one Attack Action</strong>, but does <strong>not</strong> limit your movement.
      You may move normally after standing.<br>
      Standing up itself does not provoke Reactions; only the movement you take afterward might.</li>
</ul>

---

:

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
#### Escape Rule
<em>(Leaving combat entirely.)</em>

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>
    <strong>Flee</strong> – If you choose to escape rather than fight, you move up to <strong>4 × your normal Speed</strong> directly away from danger.<br>
    You cannot make any <strong>Attacks</strong>, take <strong>Reactions</strong>, or spend <strong>Stones</strong> until the start of your next turn.<br>
    This movement <strong>provokes Reactions</strong> as normal (unless you used <strong>Disengage</strong> this turn).<br>
    The GM may immediately transition into a <strong>chase / escape resolution</strong> if appropriate.
  </li>
</ul>

---
:
#### Defensive Reactions
  <strong>Dive for Cover</strong> – After an AoE Attack successfully reaches its Area TN, but before its damage or payload is applied, a creature inside the area may spend its Reaction to immediately move up to 2 × Mastery Rank meters.

If this movement takes the creature completely outside the AoE, it is not affected by that AoE.

If it remains inside the area, it is affected normally.

This movement does not provoke Reactions.
</li>
</ul>

---


:

#### Initiative: Delay
In certain situations, you may delay your turn to react to the flow of combat.

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Trigger:</strong> At the start of your turn.</li>
  <li><strong>Effect:</strong> You skip your turn and may act immediately after any other creature finishes its turn.</li>
  <li><strong>New Initiative:</strong> Your Initiative permanently changes to the position you delayed into.</li>
  <li><strong>Delaying into the next round:</strong> If you delay past the round boundary, your delayed turn carries into the next round. You may take it after any creature finishes its turn, but you still may not interrupt another creature’s turn.</li>
  <li><strong>If you were last to act:</strong> Your new Initiative becomes <em>(highest Initiative + 1)</em>.</li>
</ul>

<strong>Restrictions:</strong>
<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li>You may not interrupt another creature’s turn.</li>
  <li>If you delay past your next natural turn, you lose that turn.</li>
  <li>You cannot delay if <strong>incapacitated</strong> or <strong>surprised</strong>.</li>
</ul>

---




}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


### Rest & Recovery 

The Mastery System is balanced around **combat rounds** as the main resource economy.  
Outside of combat, recovery is **slow, deliberate, and story-driven**.

---

#### Healing Core Rules
- **Health Bars** are your injury levels (Healthy → … → Wounded → Incapacitated).  
- A Bar becomes **Scarred** when **all boxes in that Bar are filled with damage**.  
- **Normal healing** (spells, powers, potions, medicine, auras) can remove damage **only inside your current active Health Bar**. It cannot restore or erase a fully filled (**Scarred**) Health Bar unless a rule explicitly says it restores a Scarred Bar.


> *You can patch a wound, but you cannot undo a battle without time.*

---

#### Stone Flow (Combat Economy)

- When you use a Stone, it becomes **Exhausted** (unless an effect says it becomes Sealed/Bound).
- **End of each Round:** move **MR Stones** from Exhausted → Ready.
- **End of Combat:** all remaining Exhausted Stones return to Ready, but Sealed / Bound Stones do not return by Regeneration or end-of-combat.

---

### Rests


#### 1) Night Rest (8 Hours) — Anywhere
A Night Rest is **8 hours** of downtime (sleep + light activity).

**Benefits**
- A Night Rest restores your current active Health Bar to full (boxes only). 
- A Night Rest **does not** restore Scarred Bars and does not refresh Daily/Sealed.
- A Night Rest **does not** refresh Daily resources.  
- A Night Rest **does not** restore Scarred Bars by itself.

---

#### 2) Day of Rest (24 Hours) — Natural Recovery
Without magic, your body and spirit heal over **days**, not minutes.

- Each full **day of rest** restores **1 Scarred Health Bar**.  
- Light activity (traveling, hunting, etc.) halves this rate (2 days for 1 Bar).  
- You cannot recover while **Lacerate or Blight** unless treated.

---


#### 3) Safe Haven Rest
A Safe Haven Rest is a **Night Rest** taken in a **Safe Haven** (secure + comfortable + uninterrupted).

**Benefits**
1) **Daily Reset:** you refresh all “once per day” resources:
   - **Mastery Charges** (Charged Tag) return to full.  
   - Any Stones **lost until Safe Haven Rest** return (e.g., Stones burned for daily effects).
   - Ritual Stones that were **Sealed** become available again.
2) **Form Release:** Stones bound by **Stone-Bound Form** are released.
3) **Counts as Natural Recovery:** you restore **1 Scarred Health Bar** (as a Day of Rest).
4) **Restores** your current active Health Bar to full (boxes only).

**Limits**
- You can benefit from **only one Safe Haven Rest per 24 hours**.
- A Safe Haven is a **reward and a location**, not merely “we lay down somewhere”.

---
::

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Daily Resources

---

#### Charged (Daily)
You have **Mastery Charges = your Mastery Rank**.  
Charges refresh only when you complete a **Safe Haven Rest**.

---

#### Rituals (Sealed Stones)
When you perform a Ritual, spent Stones are **Sealed** and cannot be regenerated by MR.  
They return only when you complete a **Safe Haven Rest**.

---

#### Bound Stones (Do NOT return on rest)
Stones bound into **Artifacts, Familiars, or similar permanent bonds** remain bound until released or destroyed.



}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Progression (XP)

At the end of each session, the GM awards **XP**.

XP is a single currency used for:

- **Attributes**
- **Skills**
- **Powers**
- **Artifacts**
- unlocking **Mastery Trees**
- other GM-approved progression options

You may **bank XP** and spend it later during downtime or another GM-approved Upgrade Step.

Progression in the Mastery System is intentionally slow over long campaigns.  
Characters are expected to grow across many sessions, not to rush directly toward divine power.

---

### XP Reward by Mastery Rank

| **Mastery Rank** | **Recommended XP / Session** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1–2** | **4–6 XP** |
| **MR 3** | **6–8 XP** |
| **MR 4** | **8–10 XP** |
| **MR 5** | **10–12 XP** |
| **MR 6+** | **12–14 XP** |

The GM chooses the exact XP reward based on session impact, danger, progress, roleplay, discovery, and story relevance.

A quiet session, travel session, investigation-heavy session, or mostly social session may grant the lower value.  
A dangerous session, major victory, important discovery, boss fight, or story milestone may grant the higher value.

---

### Upgrade Steps

XP is spent during an **Upgrade Step**.

An Upgrade Step usually happens during downtime, after a session, during a Safe Haven Rest, or whenever the GM allows character progression.

During each Upgrade Step, each individual upgrade may be increased only once.

This means:

- You may increase several different Attributes.
- You may increase several different Skills.
- You may increase several different Powers.
- You may increase several different Artifacts.
- You may not increase the same Attribute twice in the same Upgrade Step.
- You may not increase the same Skill twice in the same Upgrade Step.
- You may not increase the same Power twice in the same Upgrade Step.
- You may not increase the same Artifact twice in the same Upgrade Step.

This rule replaces the old Attribute 50% spending limit.

There is no longer a fixed percentage limit on Attribute spending.  
Instead, progression is controlled by the Upgrade Step limit, XP costs, Stone thresholds, and the natural need to invest in Skills, Powers, Artifacts, and other options.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Example: Legal Upgrade Step

A character spends XP during downtime.

They may choose:

- **Might** +1
- **Agility** +1
- **Athletics** +1
- **Dodge** +1
- **Main Attack Power** +1 Level
- **Armor Artifact** +1 Level

This is legal because each individual value was increased only once.

---

#### Example: Illegal Upgrade Step

A character cannot spend XP to do this in one Upgrade Step:

- **Might** +1
- **Might** +1 again
- **Might** +1 again

Even if the character has enough banked XP, the same individual Attribute cannot be increased more than once in the same Upgrade Step.

The player may save XP and increase Might again in a later Upgrade Step.

---

### Attribute and Skill Costs

Attributes and Skills use the same XP cost table.

| **New Value** | **Cost per +1 Increase** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **1–8** | **1 XP** |
| **9–16** | **2 XP** |
| **17–24** | **3 XP** |
| **25–32** | **4 XP** |
| **33–40** | **5 XP** |
| **41–48** | **6 XP** |
| **49–56** | **7 XP** |
| **57–64** | **8 XP** |
| **65–72** | **9 XP** |
| **73–80** | **10 XP** |

Use the new value to determine the cost.

For example:

- Increasing a Skill from 7 to 8 costs **1 XP**.
- Increasing a Skill from 8 to 9 costs **2 XP**.
- Increasing an Attribute from 15 to 16 costs **2 XP**.
- Increasing an Attribute from 16 to 17 costs **3 XP**.

---

### Attribute Cap

No Attribute can normally exceed **80**.

Since a character has seven Attributes and each Attribute can hold up to ten Stone milestones, the normal maximum Stone Pool is **70 Stones**.

This is the absolute upper limit of normal character progression.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Stone Milestones

Stones are gained automatically whenever an Attribute reaches **8** or a multiple of **8**.

| **Attribute Value** | **Total Stones in that Attribute** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **8** | **1 Stone** |
| **16** | **2 Stones** |
| **24** | **3 Stones** |
| **32** | **4 Stones** |
| **40** | **5 Stones** |
| **48** | **6 Stones** |
| **56** | **7 Stones** |
| **64** | **8 Stones** |
| **72** | **9 Stones** |
| **80** | **10 Stones** |

Stones are both a resource and a measure of inner power.

Mastery Rank is based primarily on the character's total Stone Pool.

---

### Power Costs

Power Level cost equals twice the new Power Level.

| **New Power Level** | **XP Cost** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **1** | **2 XP** |
| **2** | **4 XP** |
| **3** | **6 XP** |
| **4** | **8 XP** |
| **5** | **10 XP** |
| **6** | **12 XP** |
| **7** | **14 XP** |
| **8** | **16 XP** |
| **9** | **18 XP** |
| **10** | **20 XP** |
| **11** | **22 XP** |
| **12** | **24 XP** |
| **13** | **26 XP** |
| **14** | **28 XP** |
| **15** | **30 XP** |
| **16** | **32 XP** |

A Power may only be increased by one Level during the same Upgrade Step.

---

### Power Level Cap by Mastery Rank

| **Mastery Rank** | **Maximum Power Level** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1–2** | **Level 4** |
| **MR 3** | **Level 8** |
| **MR 4** | **Level 12** |
| **MR 5+** | **Level 16** |

This keeps early characters from rushing directly into high-tier Powers while still allowing strong specialization over time.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Artifact Costs

| **Artifact Advancement** | **XP Cost** |
|:--|:--:|
| Increase one Artifact by +1 Level | **8 XP** |

An Artifact may only be increased by one Level during the same Upgrade Step.

Artifacts remain limited by Artifact Capacity, Equipment Slots, Binding rules, Echo rules, and the Artifact's own written progression.

---

## Mastery Rank Progression

Mastery Rank reflects both discipline and raw power.  
It is measured primarily through the character's growing pool of Stones.

You normally begin at **Mastery Rank 2**, representing a trained adventurer or awakened Spark.

The following thresholds are the recommended Stone-based progression for the Mastery System.

---

### Recommended Mastery Progression

| **Total Stones** | **Suggested Mastery Rank** | **Tier Description** |
|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **1–7** | **MR2 – Adept** | Awakened mortal; trained adventurer. |
| **8–13** | **MR3 – Expert** | Seasoned Spark; tactical combat options become reliable. |
| **14–20** | **MR4 – Master** | Hero-tier; multiple systems begin to work together. |
| **21–29** | **MR5 – Grandmaster** | Epic champion; exceptional mortal or supernatural elite. |
| **30–39** | **MR6 – Legend** | Demon Prince ceiling; world-threatening legendary beings. |
| **40–49** | **MR7 – Mythic** | Titans, demigods, archangels, divine remnants, and ancient horrors. |
| **50–70** | **MR8 – Godlevel** | True gods and cosmic entities. This is the highest possible Mastery Rank. |

---

### Divine Scale within MR8

MR8 covers a wide divine range.  
A being with 50 Stones and a being with 70 Stones are both godlevel, but they are not equal.

| **Total Stones** | **Divine Scale** |
|:--:|:--|
| **50–55** | Lesser God |
| **56–63** | True God |
| **64–69** | High God |
| **70** | Apex God / System Limit |

MR8 is not expected as normal campaign progression.  
It represents gods, cosmic beings, final ascensions, or campaign-ending transformations.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Shared Mastery Recommendation

For a balanced campaign, all player characters should usually share the same Mastery Rank.

Individual characters may have different exact Stone totals, different Attributes, different Skills, different Powers, and different Artifacts, but the group's actual Mastery Rank should remain synchronized unless the GM intentionally wants uneven power levels.

The group rises in Mastery Rank when:

- all characters reach the next Stone bracket,
- or the GM decides that the story, training, transformation, or campaign milestone justifies the increase.

---

### Mastery Rank Effects

Each time your Mastery Rank increases, you gain:

- access to the next Mastery Rank tier,
- increased Keep according to the core dice rules,
- increased Mastery Charges,
- stronger MR-based durations and uses,
- improved Stone recovery where applicable,
- access to higher Power Level caps.

Mastery Rank is not only a number.  
It defines how much power a character can safely express, sustain, and control.

---

### Expected Campaign Pacing

The following pacing assumes normal XP rewards, normal player behavior, investment into Skills, Powers, Artifacts, and character flavor, and no forced optimization toward Stones.

This is not a strict rule.  
It is a design expectation.

| **Target Rank** | **Expected Campaign Timing** |
|:--:|:--|
| **MR3** | Around session 10–15 |
| **MR4** | Around session 35–60 |
| **MR5** | Around session 80–130 |
| **MR6** | Around session 150–220 |
| **MR7** | Around session 220+; mythic campaign only |
| **MR8** | Godlevel, special ascension, final arc, or non-standard campaign play |

Most long campaigns are expected to end around **MR5** or **MR6**.

MR7 is not normal heroic progression.  
It is mythic play.

MR8 is not normal mythic progression.  
It is godlevel.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Design Note: Why Progression Is Slow

The Mastery System is built for long-form campaigns.

Players are not expected to spend every XP reward perfectly toward Stones.  
They will buy Skills, Powers, Artifacts, Mastery Trees, character flavor, utility, side options, and personal upgrades.

This is intentional.

Mastery Rank should not rise every few sessions.  
A new Mastery Rank should feel like a major phase of the campaign.

MR6 represents the upper limit of demon-level beings.  
MR7 represents entities such as Titans, demigods, archangels, and divine remnants.  
MR8 represents true gods.

Because of this, reaching MR6 or higher should require a very long campaign, major story events, or extraordinary transformation.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Special Effects Reference  
*“Power leaves its mark — but every mark can be broken.”*  

---

This section summarizes all **Special Effects** by **how they work mechanically**.

---
Specials do not grant an automatic Attribute Check when applied.

---
If a Power hits or a Spell is successfully cast, its Specials apply.

---
Negative value-based Specials decay at the start of the affected creature's turn.

---
Cleanse and specific rules may reduce or remove Specials.

---

#### Global Rules 

**Effect Types**  
Most entries fall into one of these types:

- **Diminishing (X→0):**  
  The effect has a stack value **X**. Reapplying the same effect usually **adds stacks**. At the start of the target’s turn, the effect **ticks** (if it has a Tick), then **decays by 1**.

- **Timed:**  
  The effect lasts for a listed duration (**1 Round**, **MR Rounds**, etc.), then ends.  
  Timed effects **do not stack** unless an entry explicitly says otherwise. Reapplying them **refreshes duration** and keeps the **higher X**.

- **Until Broken / Until Used:**  
  The effect lasts until its listed break condition is met, or until its uses are spent.  
  These effects **do not stack** unless an entry explicitly says otherwise.

- **Instant:**  
  The effect resolves immediately and does not persist unless the entry says otherwise.


---

#### Cleanse(X)

Choose one ongoing Special on a creature and reduce that Special by **X**. If the Special reaches **0**, it ends.

Cleanse can only reduce Specials that list **Cleanse: Yes**. The value of one Cleanse(X) cannot be divided between multiple Specials.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Diminishing Effects

> **Diminishing (X→0):**  
> Reapplying the same effect adds stacks (**X → X + Y**) unless stated otherwise.  
> At the **start of the affected creature’s turn**, resolve the effect’s Tick (if any), then reduce **X by 1** (**X → X−1**, minimum 0).  
> If **X reaches 0**, the effect ends.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Blight(X)** | While affected, healing you receive is reduced by **X**. At Tick, take **X Stress**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Challenge(X)** | Challenge is bound to the creature that applied it. Attack Pools for attacks that do **not** include that creature as a target are reduced by **X**, to a minimum of your **Mastery Rank**. | Same source only | Yes |
| **Corrode(X)** | Your **Armor** is reduced by **X**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Disoriented(X)** | Your **Attack Dice** and all dice pools used to notice, locate, track, or identify something are reduced by **X**, to a minimum of your **Mastery Rank**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Expose(X)** | Your **Evade** is reduced by **X**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Hex(X)** | When hit by a **Spell**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Hex**, rounded up. | Yes | Yes |
| **Lacerate(X)** | You suffer damage from movement. The first time each turn you move **more than 0 m**, take **X** damage. If you move **more than half your Speed** that turn, take **+X** damage again. If you **Sprint / Dash / otherwise exceed your normal Speed**, take **+X** damage again. | Yes | Yes |
| **Mark(X)** | After a hit, spend any amount of Mark from the target to set the minimum result of each damage die to the spent Mark value for that damage roll. Then reduce Mark by the amount spent. | Yes | Yes |
| **Regeneration(X)** | At Tick, heal **X HP**. | Yes | No |
| **Ruin(X)** | At Tick, take **X** damage. | Yes | Yes |
| **Slow(X)** | Your **Speed** is reduced by **X m**. If you do not voluntarily move at least **1 m** during your turn, take **X damage** at the end of your turn. | Yes | Yes |
| **Soulburn(X)** | All dice pools based on **Wits**, **Influence**, or **Resolve** are reduced by **X**, to a minimum of your **Mastery Rank**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Sundered(X)** | When hit by a **non-Spell attack**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Sundered**, rounded up. | Yes | Yes |
| **Weaken(X)** | All dice pools based on **Might**, **Agility**, or **Intellect** are reduced by **X**, to a minimum of your **Mastery Rank**. | Yes | Yes |

---
::

#### Timed and Fixed Control Effects

> Timed effects do **not stack** unless explicitly stated otherwise.  
> Reapplying a Timed effect refreshes its duration. If it has a numeric value, keep the **higher X**.  
> Fixed Control effects are binary and never gain an X-value.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Brace(X)** | Your **Speed becomes 0 m**. While Braced, your **Shield value is doubled** for Armor calculation. At the end of each of your turns, reduce **Brace by 1**. | Until X reaches 0 | No | No |
| **Prone** | You are knocked down. Standing up requires the normal Movement Action or Action cost. | Until you stand | No | No |
| **Stunned** | Lose your next **Attack Action** and cannot use **Reactions** until the start of your next turn. | Until the start of your next turn | No | No |

---
::

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Until Broken / Until Used Effects

**Critical is not a general Special.** It is available only through the dedicated **Active Buff: Critical** entry.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Bulwark(X)** | As a **Reaction** when hit by an attack you can perceive, reduce the attack’s **final damage by 50%** and consume **1** Bulwark. | Until used | Yes | No |
| **Immovable** | You are immune to **Push**, **Pull**, **Prone**, and forced movement while the effect lasts. | Buff Duration | No | No |
| **Root(X)** | You cannot voluntarily leave your current space. Spend an **Action**, **Movement Action**, or **Reaction** to make a **Vitality Attribute Check** against **TN 8 × source Mastery Rank**. On success, reduce Root by **1**; each Raise reduces it by **1 additional point**. | Until broken | Yes | Yes |

---
::

#### Instant Effects
Instant Specials resolve as part of the successful hit that delivered them.

They do not grant an automatic Attribute Check.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Brutal Impact(X)** | Each damage die rolled counts as **at least X**. | No | No |
| **Penetration(X)** | The attack ignores **X Armor**. | No | No |
| **Precision(X)** | On hit, add **+Xd8** bonus damage. | No | No |
| **Push(X)** | Push the target **X m** immediately. | No | No |
| **Pull(X)** | Pull the target **X m** immediately. | No | No |
| **Smite(X)** | Add **+Xd8** bonus damage vs **Undead / Fiends**. | Yes | No |
| **Disarm** | On hit, the target loses grip on one visible held item; the item falls to the ground. Recovering it requires a **Movement Action** or an **Action**. | No | No |

---
::

#### Support Effects

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Cleanse(X)** | Reduce one eligible ongoing Special by **X**. The value cannot be split between multiple Specials. If the Special reaches **0**, it ends. | Instant | No |

---
:::::::

If you need the full wording for an effect, including timing, stacking, and removal details, see the **Appendix — Special Descriptions** below.



}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Equipment

Equipment is tracked through the **Inventory Grid**.

This is not a weight system.  
It is a **space and load system** that shows how much gear a character can carry while still moving and reacting cleanly.

Each item occupies a number of squares in the grid based on its listed size.

---

### Inventory Grid

Each character has an **Inventory Grid of 24 × 9 squares**.

The grid is divided into **three load zones**, each **8 columns wide**:

- **Zone 1:** Normal Load
- **Zone 2:** Encumbered
- **Zone 3:** Overloaded

The further right an item is placed, the more difficult the total load becomes to carry efficiently.

If all carried items fit completely inside **Zone 1**, the character is under **Normal Load**.

If any part of an item reaches into **Zone 2**, the character becomes **Encumbered**.

If any part of an item reaches into **Zone 3**, the character becomes **Overloaded**.

Only the **highest occupied zone** matters.

#### Load Effects

- **Normal Load:** no penalty
- **Encumbered:** **Movement −4 m**
- **Overloaded:** **Movement -6 m**

Load does not prevent carrying gear.  
It represents how difficult that gear is to carry efficiently in an active situation.

---

### Item Placement

Each item has an Inventory Size written as **width × height**.

To place an item into the Inventory Grid:

- the item must fit entirely within empty squares
- items may not overlap
- items must be placed as one continuous rectangle
- an item’s position determines whether it remains in Normal Load or pushes the character into a higher load zone

If even a single square of an item extends into a higher zone, that item counts as occupying that zone.

Item sizes are listed in the item entry, equipment list, or VTT data.

---

### Item Rotation

Items may be **rotated by 90 degrees** when placed in the Inventory Grid.

This changes the item from **width × height** to **height × width**.

Rotation is allowed only if the rotated item still fits entirely into empty squares.

---
If at least one item reaches into Zone 3, the character is **Overloaded**.
}}
